	"render": "file:./render.php"
}
Classic: Jest tests the plain DOM script directly plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/classic-accordion/test/view.test.js
/**
 * Q5, classic accordion: the viewScript is plain DOM code, so Jest (jsdom)
 * can test it directly with markup like render.php's.
 */
import { initAccordions } from '../view';

function renderAccordion() {
	document.body.innerHTML = `
		<div>
			<button type="button" class="news-uk-accordion-classic__toggle" aria-expanded="true" aria-controls="panel-1">Key facts</button>
			<div id="panel-1" class="news-uk-accordion-classic__panel">Polls close at 10pm.</div>
		</div>`;
	return {
		button: document.querySelector( 'button' ),
		panel: document.getElementById( 'panel-1' ),
	};
}

describe( 'Classic accordion viewScript', () => {
	it( 'closes the server-rendered open panel once it runs', () => {
		const { button, panel } = renderAccordion();

		initAccordions();

		expect( button.getAttribute( 'aria-expanded' ) ).toBe( 'false' );
		expect( panel.hidden ).toBe( true );
	} );

	it( 'toggles on click and keeps aria-expanded in sync', () => {
		const { button, panel } = renderAccordion();
		initAccordions();

		button.click();
		expect( button.getAttribute( 'aria-expanded' ) ).toBe( 'true' );
		expect( panel.hidden ).toBe( false );

		button.click();
		expect( panel.hidden ).toBe( true );
	} );

	it( 'is safe to call twice (no double listeners)', () => {
		const { button, panel } = renderAccordion();
		initAccordions();
		initAccordions();

		button.click();

		// With two listeners the panel would open and close again.
		expect( panel.hidden ).toBe( false );
	} );
} );
PHPUnit: server HTML for both versions plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/tests/class-accordion-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q5: the server-rendered HTML is already correct before any JS runs.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Accordion tests.
 */
class Accordion_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Directives are processed on the server: panel hidden, button not expanded.
	 */
	public function test_server_renders_closed_state() {
		$html = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/accordion {"label":"Key facts","body":"Polls close at 10pm."} /-->' );

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'data-wp-interactive="news-uk/accordion"', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'aria-expanded="false"', $html );
		// Check the panel's attributes with the HTML API: attribute order is not
		// guaranteed (the server puts "hidden" first), so don't regex HTML.
		$tags = new \WP_HTML_Tag_Processor( $html );
		$tags->next_tag(
			[
				'tag_name'   => 'div',
				'class_name' => null,
			] 
		); // Wrapper.
		$tags->next_tag( 'div' );                                            // Panel.
		$this->assertStringStartsWith( 'news-uk-accordion-', $tags->get_attribute( 'id' ) );
		$this->assertTrue( $tags->get_attribute( 'hidden' ), 'Panel is hidden in the server HTML.' );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'Polls close at 10pm.', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * The view script module is enqueued only when the block is on the page.
	 */
	public function test_view_module_is_registered_for_block() {
		$block = \WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( 'news-uk/accordion' );

		$this->assertNotEmpty( $block->view_script_module_ids );
	}

	/**
	 * Classic version: server HTML is the OPEN state (works without JS), with
	 * plain hooks for the script and no data-wp-* directives.
	 */
	public function test_classic_accordion_renders_open_html_for_its_script() {
		$html = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/accordion-classic {"label":"Key facts","body":"Polls close at 10pm."} /-->' );

		$tags = new \WP_HTML_Tag_Processor( $html );
		$tags->next_tag( 'button' );
		$this->assertTrue( $tags->has_class( 'news-uk-accordion-classic__toggle' ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'true', $tags->get_attribute( 'aria-expanded' ) );
		$panel_id = $tags->get_attribute( 'aria-controls' );

		$tags->next_tag( 'div' );
		$this->assertSame( $panel_id, $tags->get_attribute( 'id' ) );
		$this->assertNull( $tags->get_attribute( 'hidden' ), 'Visible until the script runs.' );
		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( 'data-wp-', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * Classic version: its plain viewScript is registered for the front end.
	 */
	public function test_classic_view_script_is_registered() {
		$block = \WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( 'news-uk/accordion-classic' );

		$this->assertNotEmpty( $block->view_script_handles );
		$this->assertEmpty( $block->view_script_module_ids );
	}
}
Playwright: both behave the same for a reader plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/e2e/accordion.spec.js
/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
const { test, expect } = require( '@wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright' );

test.describe( 'Q5 Accordion (Interactivity API)', () => {
	test.afterAll( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
		await requestUtils.deleteAllPosts();
	} );

	test( 'toggles open and closed for a reader', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		const post = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			title: 'Accordion test',
			status: 'publish',
			content:
				'<!-- wp:news-uk/accordion {"label":"Key facts","body":"Polls close at 10pm."} /-->',
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ post.id }` );
		const button = page.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Key facts' } );
		const panel = page.getByText( 'Polls close at 10pm.' );

		await expect( button ).toHaveAttribute( 'aria-expanded', 'false' );
		await expect( panel ).toBeHidden();

		await button.click();
		await expect( button ).toHaveAttribute( 'aria-expanded', 'true' );
		await expect( panel ).toBeVisible();

		await button.press( 'Enter' );
		await expect( panel ).toBeHidden();
	} );

	test( 'classic viewScript version behaves the same for a reader', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		const post = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			title: 'Classic accordion test',
			status: 'publish',
			content:
				'<!-- wp:news-uk/accordion-classic {"label":"Key facts","body":"Polls close at 10pm."} /-->',
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ post.id }` );
		const button = page.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Key facts' } );
		const panel = page.getByText( 'Polls close at 10pm.' );

		// The script closed the server-rendered open panel.
		await expect( button ).toHaveAttribute( 'aria-expanded', 'false' );
		await expect( panel ).toBeHidden();

		await button.click();
		await expect( panel ).toBeVisible();

		await button.press( 'Enter' );
		await expect( panel ).toBeHidden();
	} );
} );
Store anatomy: view.js, state, getters, actions, callbacks plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/save-for-later/view.js
/**
 * Q5 follow-ups: a store that uses every standard part.
 *
 * store( namespace, { state, actions, callbacks } )
 * - state:     shared by every block in this namespace on the page. PHP sets
 *              the starting values (wp_interactivity_state in render.php), and
 *              this definition is MERGED with them.
 * - getters:   derived state. Computed from other state (and context), so it
 *              can't get out of sync. PHP has matching closures for the first HTML.
 * - actions:   run on events (data-wp-on--*). Async ones are generators.
 * - callbacks: side effects (data-wp-init, data-wp-watch, data-wp-run).
 *
 * Not in the store, but used with it:
 * - getContext(): the data-wp-context of the block that triggered the code.
 * - getElement(): the DOM element the directive is on.
 * - getConfig():  read-only settings from wp_interactivity_config().
 *
 * The directive values in render.php ("actions.toggle", "state.count") are
 * just paths into this object. Nothing checks them at build time: a typo only
 * shows up in the browser, which is why there is a Playwright test.
 */
import {
	store,
	getContext,
	getElement,
	getConfig,
} from '@wordpress/interactivity';

/**
 * Turn the excerpt's HTML into plain text (it's shown with data-wp-text).
 *
 * @param {string} html Rendered excerpt.
 * @return {string} Text only.
 */
function toText( html ) {
	return (
		new window.DOMParser().parseFromString( html, 'text/html' ).body
			.textContent || ''
	).trim();
}

// Set by restore(), so persist() doesn't overwrite saved data with the empty
// server list before it has been read. Module-level, so it is shared by all
// instances, like the state.
let restored = false;

// store() returns the merged store. Keep `state` to use inside the getters
// and actions (it's the same reactive object the directives read).
const { state } = store( 'news-uk/saved', {
	state: {
		// savedIds and labels come from PHP; no need to repeat them here.

		// Derived state: a getter. Directives read it like a normal value
		// (data-wp-text="state.count"), and it updates when savedIds changes.
		get count() {
			return state.savedIds.length;
		},
		// A getter can read context too. Same getter, different answer per
		// block: each block asks about its own postId.
		get isSaved() {
			return state.savedIds.includes( getContext().postId );
		},
		get buttonLabel() {
			return state.isSaved ? state.labels.saved : state.labels.save;
		},
	},

	actions: {
		// A plain action. Change state; every directive that reads it updates,
		// in THIS block and in every other block in the namespace.
		toggle() {
			const { postId } = getContext();
			// Assign a new array rather than push(): clearer, and works the
			// same in every runtime version.
			state.savedIds = state.isSaved
				? state.savedIds.filter( ( id ) => id !== postId )
				: [ ...state.savedIds, postId ];
		},

		// An async action is a GENERATOR: `yield` where you'd write `await`.
		// The runtime resumes it with the block's scope restored, so
		// getContext() after a yield still means the block that was clicked.
		// With async/await the scope is lost after the first await.
		*loadSummary() {
			const context = getContext();
			context.loading = true;
			try {
				const { restUrl } = getConfig();
				const response = yield window.fetch(
					`${ restUrl }${ context.postId }?_fields=excerpt`
				);
				if ( ! response.ok ) {
					throw new Error( `HTTP ${ response.status }` );
				}
				const post = yield response.json();
				context.summary = toText( post.excerpt.rendered );
			} catch {
				context.summary = 'Summary unavailable.';
			} finally {
				context.loading = false;
				context.loaded = true;
			}
		},
	},

	callbacks: {
		// data-wp-init: runs once per block when it starts. The server
		// couldn't see localStorage, so load the reader's list now.
		restore() {
			if ( restored ) {
				return;
			}
			restored = true;
			try {
				const saved = JSON.parse(
					window.localStorage.getItem( getConfig().storageKey ) ||
						'[]'
				);
				if ( Array.isArray( saved ) ) {
					state.savedIds = saved.filter( Number.isInteger );
				}
			} catch {
				// Storage blocked or bad JSON: keep the server's empty list.
			}
		},

		// data-wp-watch: runs on start, then again whenever a value it READ
		// changes. It reads state.savedIds, so every save/unsave re-runs it.
		persist() {
			// Read BEFORE the early return: a watch only re-runs for values it
			// read, so returning first would mean it never subscribes.
			// eslint-disable-next-line @wordpress/no-unused-vars-before-return -- Deliberate, see above.
			const json = JSON.stringify( state.savedIds );
			if ( ! restored ) {
				return;
			}
			try {
				window.localStorage.setItem( getConfig().storageKey, json );
			} catch {
				// Storage full or blocked: saving still works for this page view.
			}
		},

		// getElement(): the element this directive is on. When the summary
		// arrives, move keyboard focus to it so screen readers announce it.
		focusSummary() {
			const { ref } = getElement();
			if ( getContext().loaded && ref ) {
				ref.focus();
			}
		},
	},
} );
Store anatomy: render.php, context, config and derived state in PHP plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/save-for-later/render.php
<?php
/**
 * Q5 follow-ups: server render for news-uk/save-for-later.
 *
 * A "Save for later" button for the current article, plus a count of saved
 * articles and a "Show summary" button that fetches the excerpt. It uses every
 * part of an Interactivity API store; view.js has the client half.
 *
 * Where each value lives, and why:
 *
 * | Value              | Lives in | Why                                          |
 * |--------------------|----------|----------------------------------------------|
 * | savedIds, labels   | state    | Shared by every instance on the page.        |
 * | count, isSaved,    | state    | Derived (getters in JS, closures in PHP), so |
 * | buttonLabel        | (derived)| they can never get out of sync with savedIds.|
 * | postId, summary,   | context  | Belongs to ONE instance.                     |
 * | loading, loaded    |          |                                              |
 * | restUrl, storageKey| config   | Read-only settings; never change in the page.|
 *
 * @var array    $attributes Block attributes (none).
 * @var string   $content    Inner content (unused).
 * @var WP_Block $block      Block instance.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

// postId comes from block context: the Query Loop's Post Template provides it
// per post, and a single post's template provides it for that post.
$news_uk_post_id = (int) ( $block->context['postId'] ?? 0 );
if ( ! $news_uk_post_id ) {
	return;
}

/*
 * Config: read-only settings for the store, read in JS with getConfig().
 * Printed once in the page's interactivity data, not per block.
 */
wp_interactivity_config(
	'news-uk/saved',
	[
		'restUrl'    => rest_url( 'wp/v2/posts/' ),
		'storageKey' => 'news-uk-saved',
	]
);

/*
 * State: shared by every block in the "news-uk/saved" namespace on the page.
 * Calling this again from a second instance MERGES, so it's safe per render.
 *
 * - savedIds starts empty: the server can't see the reader's localStorage, so
 *   the button may change from "Save for later" to "Saved" once JS runs. For a
 *   logged-in reader you'd load their list from user meta here instead, and
 *   the first HTML would already be right.
 * - labels are translated in PHP, so view.js has no hard-coded English.
 * - count, isSaved and buttonLabel are DERIVED state. In JS they are getters;
 *   here they are closures, so WordPress can work out the same values while it
 *   processes the directives (WordPress 6.7+). Inside a closure,
 *   wp_interactivity_state() and wp_interactivity_get_context() with no
 *   arguments read the current namespace and the current block's context.
 */
wp_interactivity_state(
	'news-uk/saved',
	[
		'savedIds'    => [],
		'labels'      => [
			'save'  => __( 'Save for later', 'news-uk' ),
			'saved' => __( 'Saved', 'news-uk' ),
		],
		'count'       => static function () {
			return count( wp_interactivity_state()['savedIds'] );
		},
		'isSaved'     => static function () {
			$context = wp_interactivity_get_context();
			return in_array( $context['postId'], wp_interactivity_state()['savedIds'], true );
		},
		'buttonLabel' => static function () {
			$state    = wp_interactivity_state();
			$is_saved = in_array( wp_interactivity_get_context()['postId'], $state['savedIds'], true );
			return $is_saved ? $state['labels']['saved'] : $state['labels']['save'];
		},
	]
);

/*
 * Directives used below (each points at a part of the store in view.js):
 *
 * data-wp-init="callbacks.restore"        run once when the block starts
 * data-wp-watch="callbacks.persist"       re-run whenever what it reads changes
 * data-wp-on--click="actions.toggle"      an action, on an event
 * data-wp-bind--aria-pressed="state.isSaved"  a derived value (getter)
 * data-wp-text="state.count"              text kept in sync with state
 * data-wp-bind--disabled="context.loading"    this instance's own value
 * data-wp-watch="callbacks.focusSummary"  uses getElement() to focus itself
 */
?>
<div
	<?php echo get_block_wrapper_attributes(); // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped -- Escaped by core. ?>
	data-wp-interactive="news-uk/saved"
	<?php
	// Context: this instance only. Two blocks for two posts each have their own.
	echo wp_interactivity_data_wp_context( // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped -- Escaped by core.
		[
			'postId'  => $news_uk_post_id,
			'summary' => '',
			'loading' => false,
			'loaded'  => false,
		]
	);
	?>
	data-wp-init="callbacks.restore"
	data-wp-watch="callbacks.persist"
>
	<button
		type="button"
		class="news-uk-save__toggle"
		data-wp-on--click="actions.toggle"
		data-wp-bind--aria-pressed="state.isSaved"
		data-wp-text="state.buttonLabel"
	></button>

	<p class="news-uk-save__count" aria-live="polite">
		<?php esc_html_e( 'Saved articles:', 'news-uk' ); ?>
		<span data-wp-text="state.count"></span>
	</p>

	<button
		type="button"
		class="news-uk-save__summary-toggle"
		data-wp-on--click="actions.loadSummary"
		data-wp-bind--disabled="context.loading"
		data-wp-bind--hidden="context.loaded"
	><?php esc_html_e( 'Show summary', 'news-uk' ); ?></button>

	<p
		class="news-uk-save__summary"
		tabindex="-1"
		data-wp-bind--hidden="!context.loaded"
		data-wp-text="context.summary"
		data-wp-watch="callbacks.focusSummary"
	></p>
</div>
Store anatomy: Jest with the runtime mocked plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/save-for-later/test/view.test.js
/**
 * Q5 follow-ups: unit tests for the "news-uk/saved" store.
 *
 * The store's parts are plain functions, so they can be tested without the
 * real runtime. @wordpress/interactivity is mocked with a tiny fake that:
 * - merges the store definition with "server" state (like the real store),
 * - lets each test choose what getContext/getConfig/getElement return.
 * The real runtime (directives, reactivity) is covered by PHPUnit (server
 * HTML) and Playwright (in the browser).
 */

let mockContext;
let mockElement;
const mockConfig = {
	restUrl: '/wp-json/wp/v2/posts/',
	storageKey: 'test-saved',
};

// `virtual: true`: the package only exports an ES module for browsers, which
// Jest can't resolve, and the fake replaces it anyway.
jest.mock(
	'@wordpress/interactivity',
	() => ( {
		store: ( namespace, { state, ...rest } ) => {
			// State that PHP's wp_interactivity_state() would have sent.
			const merged = {
				savedIds: [],
				labels: { save: 'Save for later', saved: 'Saved' },
			};
			// Copy getters as getters, as the real store's merge does.
			Object.defineProperties(
				merged,
				Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptors( state )
			);
			return { state: merged, ...rest };
		},
		getContext: () => mockContext,
		getConfig: () => mockConfig,
		getElement: () => mockElement,
	} ),
	{ virtual: true }
);

// Load a fresh copy of view.js per test (it has module-level state).
function loadStore() {
	let result;
	jest.isolateModules( () => {
		const interactivity = require( '@wordpress/interactivity' );
		const spy = jest.spyOn( interactivity, 'store' );
		require( '../view' );
		result = spy.mock.results[ 0 ].value;
	} );
	return result;
}

beforeEach( () => {
	window.localStorage.clear();
	mockContext = { postId: 1, summary: '', loading: false, loaded: false };
	mockElement = { ref: document.createElement( 'p' ) };
} );

describe( 'state getters (derived state)', () => {
	it( 'count follows savedIds', () => {
		const { state } = loadStore();
		expect( state.count ).toBe( 0 );
		state.savedIds = [ 1, 2 ];
		expect( state.count ).toBe( 2 );
	} );

	it( 'isSaved and buttonLabel combine shared state with each block’s context', () => {
		const { state } = loadStore();
		state.savedIds = [ 2 ];

		mockContext = { postId: 1 };
		expect( state.isSaved ).toBe( false );
		expect( state.buttonLabel ).toBe( 'Save for later' );

		mockContext = { postId: 2 };
		expect( state.isSaved ).toBe( true );
		expect( state.buttonLabel ).toBe( 'Saved' );
	} );
} );

describe( 'actions', () => {
	it( 'toggle adds and removes the clicked block’s post', () => {
		const { state, actions } = loadStore();
		actions.toggle();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [ 1 ] );

		// Another block (post 2) changes the SAME shared list.
		mockContext = { postId: 2 };
		actions.toggle();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [ 1, 2 ] );

		mockContext = { postId: 1 };
		actions.toggle();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [ 2 ] );
	} );

	it( 'loadSummary is a generator: each yield is a step the runtime awaits', () => {
		const { actions } = loadStore();
		const fetchSpy = jest.fn( () => 'fetch-promise' );
		window.fetch = fetchSpy;

		// Drive the generator by hand, as the runtime would.
		const steps = actions.loadSummary();
		expect( steps.next().value ).toBe( 'fetch-promise' );
		expect( fetchSpy ).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
			'/wp-json/wp/v2/posts/1?_fields=excerpt'
		);
		expect( mockContext.loading ).toBe( true );

		const response = { ok: true, json: () => 'json-promise' };
		expect( steps.next( response ).value ).toBe( 'json-promise' );

		const done = steps.next( {
			excerpt: { rendered: '<p>Polls close at <b>10pm</b>.</p>' },
		} );
		expect( done.done ).toBe( true );
		expect( mockContext ).toMatchObject( {
			summary: 'Polls close at 10pm.',
			loading: false,
			loaded: true,
		} );
	} );

	it( 'loadSummary shows a fallback when the request fails', () => {
		const { actions } = loadStore();
		window.fetch = jest.fn();
		const steps = actions.loadSummary();
		steps.next();
		steps.next( { ok: false, status: 404 } );
		expect( mockContext ).toMatchObject( {
			summary: 'Summary unavailable.',
			loading: false,
			loaded: true,
		} );
	} );
} );

describe( 'callbacks', () => {
	it( 'restore loads the saved list once; persist writes changes back', () => {
		window.localStorage.setItem( 'test-saved', '[3,4]' );
		const { state, actions, callbacks } = loadStore();

		// persist runs before restore can: it must not wipe storage.
		callbacks.persist();
		expect( window.localStorage.getItem( 'test-saved' ) ).toBe( '[3,4]' );

		callbacks.restore();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [ 3, 4 ] );

		actions.toggle(); // Post 1.
		callbacks.persist(); // The runtime re-runs the watch after the change.
		expect( window.localStorage.getItem( 'test-saved' ) ).toBe( '[3,4,1]' );

		// A second block's init does nothing (already restored).
		window.localStorage.setItem( 'test-saved', '[]' );
		callbacks.restore();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [ 3, 4, 1 ] );
	} );

	it( 'restore ignores bad stored data', () => {
		window.localStorage.setItem( 'test-saved', '{not json' );
		const { state, callbacks } = loadStore();
		callbacks.restore();
		expect( state.savedIds ).toEqual( [] );
	} );

	it( 'focusSummary uses getElement() to focus the summary once loaded', () => {
		const { callbacks } = loadStore();
		document.body.appendChild( mockElement.ref );
		mockElement.ref.tabIndex = -1;

		callbacks.focusSummary();
		expect( document.activeElement ).not.toBe( mockElement.ref );

		mockContext.loaded = true;
		callbacks.focusSummary();
		expect( document.activeElement ).toBe( mockElement.ref );
	} );
} );
Store anatomy: PHPUnit, derived state rendered on the server plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/tests/class-save-for-later-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q5 follow-ups: the "news-uk/saved" store, server side.
 *
 * WordPress processes the directives in PHP, including derived state (the
 * closures in render.php), so the first HTML already has the right label,
 * count and aria-pressed. These tests read that HTML with the HTML API.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_HTML_Tag_Processor;
use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Save for later tests.
 */
class Save_For_Later_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Post the block belongs to.
	 *
	 * @var int
	 */
	private $post_id;

	/**
	 * Fresh Interactivity API data per test (state, config are per request).
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		$GLOBALS['wp_interactivity'] = new \WP_Interactivity_API();
		$this->post_id               = self::factory()->post->create();
	}

	/**
	 * Render the block as if it were inside this post (postId context).
	 *
	 * @return string HTML.
	 */
	private function render() {
		$block = new \WP_Block(
			[
				'blockName'    => 'news-uk/save-for-later',
				'attrs'        => [],
				'innerBlocks'  => [],
				'innerHTML'    => '',
				'innerContent' => [],
			],
			[ 'postId' => $this->post_id ]
		);
		return $block->render();
	}

	/**
	 * Read the first matching tag's attribute and following text.
	 *
	 * @param string $html  HTML.
	 * @param string $class_name Class name.
	 * @return WP_HTML_Tag_Processor Positioned on the tag.
	 */
	private function find( $html, $class_name ) {
		$tags = new WP_HTML_Tag_Processor( $html );
		$this->assertTrue( $tags->next_tag( [ 'class_name' => $class_name ] ), "Found .$class_name" );
		return $tags;
	}

	/**
	 * Context, init/watch callbacks and the store namespace are in the markup.
	 */
	public function test_markup_points_at_the_store() {
		$html = $this->render();
		$tags = new WP_HTML_Tag_Processor( $html );
		$tags->next_tag( 'div' );

		$this->assertSame( 'news-uk/saved', $tags->get_attribute( 'data-wp-interactive' ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'callbacks.restore', $tags->get_attribute( 'data-wp-init' ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'callbacks.persist', $tags->get_attribute( 'data-wp-watch' ) );
		$context = json_decode( $tags->get_attribute( 'data-wp-context' ), true );
		$this->assertSame( $this->post_id, $context['postId'] );
		$this->assertFalse( $context['loaded'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Derived state is worked out on the server: nothing saved yet.
	 */
	public function test_server_renders_derived_state_for_an_empty_list() {
		$html = $this->render();

		$button = $this->find( $html, 'news-uk-save__toggle' );
		$this->assertSame( 'false', $button->get_attribute( 'aria-pressed' ) );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '>Save for later</button>', $html );
		$this->assertMatchesRegularExpression( '#data-wp-text="state.count">0</span>#', $html );

		// The summary is hidden until loaded (context.loaded is false).
		$summary = $this->find( $html, 'news-uk-save__summary' );
		$this->assertTrue( $summary->get_attribute( 'hidden' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * If PHP knows the list (e.g. from user meta), the first HTML is right:
	 * the closures read the shared state and this block's context.
	 */
	public function test_server_renders_derived_state_for_a_saved_post() {
		wp_interactivity_state( 'news-uk/saved', [ 'savedIds' => [ $this->post_id, 999 ] ] );
		$html = $this->render();

		$button = $this->find( $html, 'news-uk-save__toggle' );
		$this->assertSame( 'true', $button->get_attribute( 'aria-pressed' ) );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '>Saved</button>', $html );
		$this->assertMatchesRegularExpression( '#data-wp-text="state.count">2</span>#', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * State and config are registered for the client (getConfig, store state).
	 */
	public function test_state_and_config_are_sent_to_the_client() {
		$this->render();

		$config = wp_interactivity_config( 'news-uk/saved' );
		$this->assertSame( rest_url( 'wp/v2/posts/' ), $config['restUrl'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'news-uk-saved', $config['storageKey'] );

		$state = wp_interactivity_state( 'news-uk/saved' );
		$this->assertSame( [], $state['savedIds'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'Saved', $state['labels']['saved'] );
		$this->assertInstanceOf( \Closure::class, $state['count'], 'Derived state is a closure in PHP.' );
	}

	/**
	 * A second instance merges into the same state instead of resetting it.
	 */
	public function test_second_instance_keeps_shared_state() {
		wp_interactivity_state( 'news-uk/saved', [ 'savedIds' => [ 5 ] ] );
		$this->render();
		$this->render();
		$this->assertSame( [ 5 ], wp_interactivity_state( 'news-uk/saved' )['savedIds'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Without a post (no postId context), render nothing.
	 */
	public function test_renders_nothing_without_post_context() {
		$this->assertSame( '', do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/save-for-later /-->' ) );
	}
}
Store anatomy: Playwright, shared state, async action, init and watch plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q05-interactivity/e2e/save-for-later.spec.js
/**
 * Q5 follow-ups: the "news-uk/saved" store in a real browser.
 *
 * Checks what the unit tests can't: that the directive paths in render.php
 * really match the store (a typo would only fail here), that shared state
 * updates every block, and the async action, init and watch callbacks.
 */
const { test, expect } = require( '@wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright' );

test.describe( 'Q5 Save for later (store anatomy)', () => {
	test.afterAll( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
		await requestUtils.deleteAllPosts();
	} );

	test( 'shared state, async action, init and watch callbacks', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		// Two instances in one post: same postId context, one shared state.
		const post = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			title: 'Save test',
			status: 'publish',
			excerpt: 'Polls close at 10pm.',
			content:
				'<!-- wp:news-uk/save-for-later /--><!-- wp:news-uk/save-for-later /-->',
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ post.id }` );
		const blocks = page.locator( '.wp-block-news-uk-save-for-later' );
		const first = blocks.nth( 0 );
		const second = blocks.nth( 1 );

		// Server-rendered derived state.
		await expect(
			first.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Save for later' } )
		).toHaveAttribute( 'aria-pressed', 'false' );
		await expect( first.getByText( 'Saved articles: 0' ) ).toBeVisible();

		// Action + shared state: clicking in the first block updates BOTH.
		await first.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Save for later' } ).click();
		for ( const block of [ first, second ] ) {
			await expect(
				block.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Saved' } )
			).toHaveAttribute( 'aria-pressed', 'true' );
			await expect(
				block.getByText( 'Saved articles: 1' )
			).toBeVisible();
		}

		// data-wp-watch (persist) wrote it; data-wp-init (restore) reads it back
		// after a reload, when the server HTML says "Save for later" again.
		await expect
			.poll( () =>
				page.evaluate( () => localStorage.getItem( 'news-uk-saved' ) )
			)
			.toBe( `[${ post.id }]` );
		await page.reload();
		await expect(
			first.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Saved' } )
		).toBeVisible();

		// Async generator action + getConfig (REST URL) + context: only the
		// clicked block loads its summary, and focus moves to it (getElement).
		await first.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Show summary' } ).click();
		const summary = first.getByText( 'Polls close at 10pm.' );
		await expect( summary ).toBeVisible();
		await expect( summary ).toBeFocused();
		await expect(
			second.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Show summary' } )
		).toBeVisible();
	} );
} );

Before the Interactivity API (WordPress 6.5), what did people do? A front-end script per block: block.json viewScript (since 5.9), or wp_enqueue_script() on every page in older code, often with jQuery. The script waits for the DOM, finds each block with querySelectorAll, reads state from the markup, adds listeners, and updates the DOM by hand. For complex widgets, some sites rendered a whole React app on the front end instead (createRoot( el ).render( <App /> ), with data passed in a data- attribute or wp_localize_script()). That shipped React to every reader and rendered nothing useful until the JS loaded.

Classic viewScriptInteractivity API
Where behaviour is describedIn JS: selectors and listenersIn the markup: data-wp-on--click, data-wp-bind--*
Keeping the DOM in syncBy hand: every change updates aria-expanded and hiddenAutomatic: change context.isOpen, the runtime updates the bindings
First paintServer HTML is open; JS closes it, so readers may see a flashThe server renders the closed state; no flash
JS sent (measured here)490 bytes, nothing shared141 bytes + the shared runtime (about 44 KB minified, about 16 KB gzipped)
Several interactive blocksEach writes its own wiringOne runtime and one pattern shared by all; blocks can share state and use client-side navigation
TestingJest on the plain DOM script is easyDirectives are tested via server HTML (PHPUnit) and the browser (Playwright)

Honest trade-off for the interview: for one small widget, the classic script sends less JavaScript. The Interactivity API pays off with several interactive blocks on a page, server-rendered state (good for SEO, the edge cache and no layout flash), and one consistent pattern for the team. Also worth saying: for a plain show/hide, native HTML <details><summary> needs no JavaScript at all.

How it's tested: PHPUnit checks both versions' server HTML (the Interactivity one already closed; the classic one open, with no directives). Jest tests the classic script on plain DOM (closes on load, toggles, safe to call twice). Playwright checks both in Chrome with the mouse and the keyboard (Enter). For "Save for later": 8 Jest tests (getters, both actions, all three callbacks), 6 PHPUnit tests (derived state on the server, context, state and config) and 1 Playwright test (two blocks sharing state, the summary fetch, saved state surviving a reload).

✓ Builds with wp-scripts✓ Jest passes✓ PHPUnit passes✓ Playwright passes (Chrome)

Q6.What is the difference between block-based themes and Full Site Editing (FSE) themes?

"In practice they're the same thing. A block theme is what makes Full Site Editing possible. The real contrast is block theme vs classic theme."

Picture this"Block theme" is the engine, "FSE" is the driving experience it gives you. Asking for the difference is like asking the difference between an electric car and electric driving.
Block (FSE) themeClassic / hybrid theme
Templates are HTML files of blocks in templates/ and parts/, plus theme.jsonTemplates are PHP files (single.php, header.php)
Full Site Editor: edit headers, footers, templates and global styles visuallyCan adopt theme.json and some block template parts, but no full Site Editor
Example"In a block theme, templates/single.html is just block markup: Post Title, Post Featured Image, Post Content. An editor can rearrange it in the Site Editor. In a classic theme the same thing is single.php, and only a developer can change it."
Remember it asSame thing, two names. Multidots default: FSE-first block themes, theme.json for tokens, patterns for layouts, custom blocks in a plugin.
Real code in this repo

📁 themes/news-uk-theme/ and plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q06-block-vs-classic-theme/

The practice theme is a block theme: HTML templates made of blocks, plus theme.json. For contrast, classic-single.php shows the same template the classic way (reference only, not loaded).

<main class="wp-block-group"><!-- wp:post-title {"level":1} /-->

<p class="news-uk-byline has-small-font-size"></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<p class="news-uk-standfirst has-large-font-size"></p>

Classic theme: single.php (developer-only) plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q06-block-vs-classic-theme/classic-single.php
<?php
/**
 * Q6 reference only, not loaded: what themes/news-uk-theme/templates/single.html
 * looks like in a classic theme. Only a developer can change this file; in the
 * block theme an editor can rearrange the same template in the Site Editor.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

get_header(); // Classic: header.php is included by PHP, not a template part block.

// The Loop. In the block theme this is implicit: the template shows the current post.
while ( have_posts() ) :
	the_post();
	?>
	<main>
		<h1><?php the_title(); ?></h1>
		<?php // Custom field read by hand. In the block theme a bound core Paragraph does this. ?>
		<p class="news-uk-standfirst"><?php echo esc_html( get_post_meta( get_the_ID(), 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) ); ?></p>
		<?php the_content(); ?>
	</main>
	<?php
endwhile;

get_footer();
PHPUnit: it really is a block theme plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q06-block-vs-classic-theme/tests/class-block-theme-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q6: the practice theme is a block theme, and its templates are block markup.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Block theme tests.
 */
class Block_Theme_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Activate the practice theme for each test.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		switch_theme( 'news-uk-theme' );
	}

	/**
	 * Block theme = templates/*.html made of blocks, plus theme.json.
	 */
	public function test_theme_is_a_block_theme() {
		$this->assertTrue( wp_is_block_theme() );
		$this->assertTrue( wp_theme_has_theme_json() );
	}

	/**
	 * The single template is block markup an editor can change in the Site Editor.
	 */
	public function test_single_template_is_block_markup() {
		$template = get_block_template( 'news-uk-theme//single' );

		$this->assertNotNull( $template );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '<!-- wp:post-title', $template->content );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '<!-- wp:post-content', $template->content );
	}

	/**
	 * Theme.json tokens become CSS custom properties.
	 */
	public function test_theme_json_outputs_css_variables() {
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '--wp--preset--color--accent: #c8102e', wp_get_global_stylesheet( [ 'variables' ] ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: The test asserts wp_is_block_theme(), that the single template is block markup, and that theme.json produces --wp--preset--color--accent.

Q7.Describe the overall FSE architecture and how you customize it.

"theme.json sets the design rules, templates and parts set the structure, patterns and blocks fill it in. To customise, I start with the lightest tool and only go heavier when I need to."

Picture thisA house. theme.json is the paint palette and building regulations. Templates and parts are the floor plan (header, footer, single post). Patterns are pre-arranged furniture sets. Blocks are the individual pieces of furniture. Bindings plug furniture into the mains (data). The Interactivity API makes the doors open.

How theme.json works: it merges four layers in order (core defaults, then parent theme, then child theme, then the user's global styles) and outputs CSS custom properties such as --wp--preset--color--primary.

Customisation ladder, lightest first:

  1. theme.json settings and styles
  2. Block styles and block variations
  3. Patterns
  4. Custom blocks
  5. PHP filters (render_block, block_type_metadata)

Custom fields without ACF: register_post_meta() with show_in_rest, then either a sidebar panel (PluginDocumentSettingPanel + useEntityProp) or a binding to a core block (core/post-meta).

Example"The Sun wants a red 'Exclusive' label on some headlines. I wouldn't build a block for that. First I'd try a block style on the core Heading ('is-style-exclusive'), set up in theme.json. That's one rung up the ladder, no JS, and editors pick it from the Styles panel."
Remember it as"Tokens, Styles, Patterns, Blocks, Filters." Climb only as high as you need.
Your storyIf you haven't shipped a block theme to production, say so plainly. Then talk about the small block theme you built for practice: you'll be speaking from experience.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q07-fse-customisation/

One real example for each rung of the ladder. Tokens live in theme.json. The block style is "Exclusive" on core Heading. The variation is a core Group preset as a "Breaking news banner". The pattern is a "Key points" box made only of core blocks. The filter uses the HTML API to add an analytics attribute to every core Button.

Block style: register_block_style with style_data plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q07-fse-customisation/load.php
function register_styles() {
	register_block_style(
		'core/heading', // Which block gets the style.
		[
			// Saved as className "is-style-exclusive" on the heading.
			'name'       => 'exclusive',
			'label'      => __( 'Exclusive', 'news-uk' ),
			// style_data (WP 6.6+) uses the same shape as theme.json "styles", so
			// WordPress generates the CSS and it can use the theme's tokens.
			// No stylesheet to write or enqueue.
			'style_data' => [
				'color'  => [ 'text' => 'var(--wp--preset--color--accent)' ],
				'border' => [
					'left' => [
						'color' => 'var(--wp--preset--color--accent)',
						'width' => '6px',
						'style' => 'solid',
					],
				],
			],
		]
	);
}
add_action( 'init', __NAMESPACE__ . '\register_styles' );
Block variation: get_block_type_variations filter plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q07-fse-customisation/load.php
function add_breaking_banner_variation( $variations, $block_type ) {
	// This filter runs for every block type: only touch core/group.
	if ( 'core/group' !== $block_type->name ) {
		return $variations;
	}

	$variations[] = [
		'name'        => 'news-uk-breaking-banner',
		'title'       => __( 'Breaking news banner', 'news-uk' ),
		'description' => __( 'A red banner for breaking news.', 'news-uk' ),
		'scope'       => [ 'inserter' ], // Show it in the inserter as if it were its own block.
		// Preset attributes: colour slugs from theme.json, so they follow the tokens.
		'attributes'  => [
			'className'       => 'news-uk-breaking-banner',
			'backgroundColor' => 'accent',
			'textColor'       => 'paper',
		],
		// What the banner starts with: a BREAKING label and an empty heading.
		'innerBlocks' => [
			[
				'core/paragraph',
				[
					'content'  => '<strong>' . __( 'BREAKING', 'news-uk' ) . '</strong>',
					'fontSize' => 'small',
				],
			],
			[ 'core/heading', [ 'level' => 2 ] ],
		],
		// How the editor recognises an existing group as this variation (to show
		// its name and icon): compare the className attribute.
		'isActive'    => [ 'className' ],
	];

	return $variations;
}
add_filter( 'get_block_type_variations', __NAMESPACE__ . '\add_breaking_banner_variation', 10, 2 );
Filter: WP_HTML_Tag_Processor on core/button plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q07-fse-customisation/load.php
function add_button_tracking( $block_content ) {
	// The HTML API parses the markup properly, so attributes are escaped and
	// existing ones are kept. A regex on HTML would break on edge cases.
	$tags = new \WP_HTML_Tag_Processor( $block_content );

	// Find the first <a> (the button link) and add the attribute to it.
	if ( $tags->next_tag( 'a' ) ) {
		$tags->set_attribute( 'data-analytics', 'cta-button' );
	}

	return $tags->get_updated_html();
}
// render_block_{name} runs only for that block type, each time it renders on
// the front end: cheaper than the generic render_block filter.
add_filter( 'render_block_core/button', __NAMESPACE__ . '\add_button_tracking' );

PHPUnit: one test per rung plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q07-fse-customisation/tests/class-fse-customisation-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q7: one test per rung of the customisation ladder.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * FSE customisation tests.
 */
class FSE_Customisation_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Block style is registered on core/heading.
	 */
	public function test_exclusive_heading_style_is_registered() {
		$styles = \WP_Block_Styles_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered_styles_for_block( 'core/heading' );

		$this->assertArrayHasKey( 'exclusive', $styles );
	}

	/**
	 * Variation is offered on core/group.
	 */
	public function test_breaking_banner_variation_is_offered() {
		$group = \WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( 'core/group' );
		$names = wp_list_pluck( $group->get_variations(), 'name' );

		$this->assertContains( 'news-uk-breaking-banner', $names );
	}

	/**
	 * Pattern is registered and made of core blocks only.
	 */
	public function test_key_points_pattern_uses_only_core_blocks() {
		$pattern = \WP_Block_Patterns_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( 'news-uk/key-points' );

		$this->assertNotNull( $pattern );
		preg_match_all( '/<!-- wp:([a-z0-9\/-]+)/', $pattern['content'], $matches );
		foreach ( $matches[1] as $block_name ) {
			$this->assertStringNotContainsString( '/', $block_name, "$block_name is not a core block" );
		}
	}

	/**
	 * Filter adds the analytics attribute without breaking the markup.
	 */
	public function test_button_filter_adds_analytics_attribute() {
		$html = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:button --><div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="/subscribe">Subscribe</a></div><!-- /wp:button -->' );

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'data-analytics="cta-button"', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'href="/subscribe"', $html );
	}
}

How it's tested: PHPUnit checks that the style, variation and pattern are registered (the pattern uses core blocks only) and that the filter keeps the link intact. The "Exclusive" style is also tested end to end in Q32.

✓ PHPUnit passes✓ Playwright passes (Chrome)

Q8.Why have you not used the core blocks, and created custom blocks instead?

"Core first is my default. I only build custom when core genuinely can't do it, and I try variations, styles, patterns and bindings first."

Picture thisBuying vs tailoring a suit. Off the rack (core) is cheaper, maintained by someone else and gets better every release. You only go to a tailor when the fit really matters.

Valid reasons to go custom (good to list on your fingers):

  1. A data source core can't express (e.g. a product catalogue API)
  2. An editorial workflow requirement
  3. Strict markup for ads, analytics or structured data (schema.org)
  4. Performance-critical rendering
  5. A locked design editors mustn't break
Remember it as"D-E-S-P-L": Data, Editorial, Strict markup, Performance, Locked design. Then: "...and before that I tried variations, styles, patterns and bindings."
Your storyZiff Davis needed strict markup for affiliate monetisation and rich-result schema. Core blocks can't guarantee exact markup, so custom was justified. Say what you tried in core first.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q08-core-first/

The one custom block in this section, and why it's justified. A Product Box needs exact markup: rel="sponsored" for affiliate rules and schema.org Product JSON-LD for rich results. Core can't guarantee either (that's the "S" in D-E-S-P-L). The core-first options (pattern, style, variation) are in Q7. The data comes from the Q25 local catalogue.

render.php: strict markup + JSON-LD plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q08-core-first/render.php
<?php
/**
 * Server-side render for news-uk/product-box.
 *
 * Exact markup matters here: rel="sponsored" for affiliate compliance and a
 * schema.org Product for rich results. That is the "Strict markup" reason in
 * D-E-S-P-L for building a custom block instead of using core.
 *
 * @var array    $attributes Block attributes.
 * @var string   $content    Inner content (unused).
 * @var WP_Block $block      Block instance.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

// Read from the LOCAL copy synced by Q25 (cached), never the external API:
// a slow or down API can't slow this page.
$news_uk_product = \News_UK_Practice\Q25\get_product( $attributes['productId'] ?? '' );

// Unknown product: render nothing rather than a broken box.
if ( ! $news_uk_product ) {
	return;
}

// schema.org Product with an Offer: what Google reads for price rich results.

$news_uk_schema = [
	'@context' => 'https://schema.org',
	'@type'    => 'Product',
	'name'     => $news_uk_product['name'],
	'offers'   => [
		'@type'         => 'Offer',
		'price'         => number_format( $news_uk_product['price'], 2, '.', '' ),
		'priceCurrency' => $news_uk_product['currency'],
		'url'           => $news_uk_product['url'],
	],
];
?>
<div <?php echo get_block_wrapper_attributes( [ 'class' => 'news-uk-product-box' ] ); // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped -- Escaped by core. ?>>
	<p class="news-uk-product-box__name"><?php echo esc_html( $news_uk_product['name'] ); ?></p>
	<p class="news-uk-product-box__price">
		<?php echo esc_html( 'GBP' === $news_uk_product['currency'] ? '£' : $news_uk_product['currency'] . ' ' ); ?><?php echo esc_html( number_format_i18n( $news_uk_product['price'], 2 ) ); ?>
	</p>
	<?php
	// rel="sponsored": required for paid/affiliate links. nofollow: no SEO credit.
	// noopener: the new tab can't control this one (security, with target=_blank).
	?>
	<a class="news-uk-product-box__buy" href="<?php echo esc_url( $news_uk_product['url'] ); ?>" rel="sponsored nofollow noopener" target="_blank">
		<?php esc_html_e( 'Buy now', 'news-uk' ); ?>
	</a>
	<?php
	// wp_json_encode escapes the data for JSON. JSON_HEX_TAG turns < and > into
	// \u003C/\u003E so a product name can never close the </script> tag (XSS).
	?>
	<script type="application/ld+json"><?php echo wp_json_encode( $news_uk_schema, JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_HEX_TAG ); ?></script>
</div>
index.js: ServerSideRender preview plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q08-core-first/index.js
/**
 * Q8: editor side of the Product Box, the one custom block that is justified.
 *
 * Note how little JS there is: one sidebar field and a server-side preview.
 * All the markup that justifies a custom block (rel="sponsored", Product
 * JSON-LD) lives in render.php, where it can't drift between editor and site.
 */
import { registerBlockType } from '@wordpress/blocks';
import { useBlockProps, InspectorControls } from '@wordpress/block-editor';
import { PanelBody, TextControl } from '@wordpress/components';
// ServerSideRender asks the REST API to run render.php and shows the result,
// so the editor preview is the real front-end HTML, not a JS copy of it.
import ServerSideRender from '@wordpress/server-side-render';
import { __ } from '@wordpress/i18n';
import metadata from './block.json';

function Edit( { attributes, setAttributes } ) {
	return (
		// useBlockProps: the block's selectable wrapper (see q01 edit.js).
		<div { ...useBlockProps() }>
			<InspectorControls>
				<PanelBody title={ __( 'Product', 'news-uk' ) }>
					{ /* The ID in the external catalogue, e.g. "iphone-16" (synced by Q25). */ }
					<TextControl
						__next40pxDefaultSize
						__nextHasNoMarginBottom
						label={ __( 'Product ID', 'news-uk' ) }
						help={ __( 'For example iphone-16', 'news-uk' ) }
						value={ attributes.productId }
						onChange={ ( productId ) =>
							setAttributes( { productId } )
						}
					/>
				</PanelBody>
			</InspectorControls>
			{ /* Preview with the real PHP output, so editor and front end match. */ }
			<ServerSideRender
				block={ metadata.name }
				attributes={ attributes }
				EmptyResponsePlaceholder={ () => (
					<p>
						{ __( 'Choose a product in the sidebar.', 'news-uk' ) }
					</p>
				) }
			/>
		</div>
	);
}

// Dynamic (Q2): the price must be live, so nothing is frozen into post_content.
registerBlockType( metadata.name, { edit: Edit, save: () => null } );
PHPUnit: the markup is the contract plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q08-core-first/tests/class-product-box-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q8: the custom block exists for its exact markup, so test the markup.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Product Box tests.
 */
class Product_Box_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Store a product locally as the sync would.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		\News_UK_Practice\Q25\save_product(
			[
				'id'       => 'iphone-16',
				'name'     => 'iPhone 16',
				'price'    => 799,
				'currency' => 'GBP',
				'url'      => 'https://shop.example.com/iphone-16',
			]
		);
	}

	/**
	 * Affiliate link carries rel="sponsored".
	 */
	public function test_affiliate_link_is_sponsored() {
		$html = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/product-box {"productId":"iphone-16"} /-->' );

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'rel="sponsored nofollow noopener"', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '£799.00', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * JSON-LD is valid and matches schema.org Product.
	 */
	public function test_outputs_valid_product_schema() {
		$html = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/product-box {"productId":"iphone-16"} /-->' );

		preg_match( '#<script type="application/ld\+json">(.*?)</script>#s', $html, $match );
		$schema = json_decode( $match[1], true );

		$this->assertSame( 'Product', $schema['@type'] );
		$this->assertSame( '799.00', $schema['offers']['price'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'GBP', $schema['offers']['priceCurrency'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Unknown product renders nothing rather than a broken box.
	 */
	public function test_unknown_product_renders_nothing() {
		$this->assertSame( '', trim( do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/product-box {"productId":"nope"} /-->' ) ) );
	}
}

Seen running: Demo post "iPhone 16 review" in Chrome: the box shows the live synced price, the link has rel="sponsored nofollow noopener", and the JSON-LD parses as a Product with price and priceCurrency: GBP.

How it's tested: PHPUnit checks the rel attribute, the price, and that the JSON-LD decodes as a valid Product.

✓ Builds with wp-scripts✓ PHPUnit passes✓ Checked in Chrome

2. PHP, testing and code quality

News UK has a minimal QA team, so engineers own the testing. Whenever you describe building something, say how you tested it.

Q9.Do you follow the coding standards regularly?

"Yes, and tooling enforces them so it doesn't depend on memory: PHPCS with WPCS and VIP rules, PHPStan, ESLint and Stylelint, running in the editor, before every commit and in CI."

Picture thisA spell-checker that won't let you send the email. Standards aren't a document you re-read, they're a gate the code has to pass four times.
ToolChecks
PHPCS + WPCS + WordPress-VIP-GoWordPress style, security (escaping, sanitising), VIP platform rules
PHPCompatibilityWPCode works on the target PHP versions
PHPStan (with WP stubs)Type bugs, calling things that don't exist
ESLint (@wordpress/eslint-plugin), StylelintJS and CSS

Four checkpoints: 1) editor, as you type; 2) pre-commit hook (husky + lint-staged); 3) CI, which blocks the merge; 4) VIP Code Analysis Bot on the PR.

Security is part of the standard: sanitise input, escape late, nonces on forms and AJAX, capability check on every privileged action.

Remember it as"Editor, Commit, CI, Bot." Four gates, so a mistake can't slip past all of them.
Your storyMention your WordPress VIP certification (2026) here, plus PHPCS/WPCS, PHPCBF and PHPStan at XWP.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q09-coding-standards/, .github/workflows/ci.yml, .husky/, phpcs.xml.dist

Three of the four gates exist in the repo (the fourth, the VIP Code Analysis Bot, only runs on VIP-hosted repos). Of the tools in the table, PHPCompatibilityWP and PHPStan aren't set up here. Editor: phpcs.xml.dist and ESLint configs. Commit: husky runs lint-staged on staged files only, with PHPCS inside the container. CI: GitHub Actions blocks the merge. A deliberately bad fixture shows PHPCS catching real problems: composer lint:demo.

fixtures/bad-example.php: six planted mistakes plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q09-coding-standards/fixtures/bad-example.php
<?php
/**
 * Q9 FIXTURE: deliberately bad code, excluded from the normal lint run.
 * Run `npm run lint:php-demo` to see PHPCS (WordPress-Extra + WordPress-VIP-Go)
 * catch each problem.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

/**
 * Prints a search results heading.
 */
function news_uk_bad_search_heading() {
	// 1. Unsanitised input, 2. no nonce, 3. unescaped output (XSS).
	echo '<h2>Results for ' . $_GET['s'] . '</h2>';

	// 4. Uncached remote call with no timeout, on every page load.
	$response = file_get_contents( 'https://api.example.com/trending' );

	// 5. Unbounded query: -1 can pull 100k posts into memory.
	$posts = get_posts( array( 'posts_per_page' => -1 ) );

	// 6. Slow core function VIP replaces with a cached version.
	$id = url_to_postid( 'https://example.com/foo' );
}

	</rule>

bin/phpcs-staged.sh: PHPCS on staged files inside Docker bin/phpcs-staged.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Q9: pre-commit PHPCS on staged files only, run inside the WordPress container.
# lint-staged passes absolute host paths; convert them to repo-relative ones.
set -euo pipefail
root="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
files=()
for f in "$@"; do files+=( "${f#"$root"/}" ); done
docker compose exec -T --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -w /var/www/html/wp-content wordpress phpcs "${files[@]}"
.github/workflows/ci.yml: the merge gate .github/workflows/ci.yml
# Q9 + Q19: the merge gate. Same Docker stack as local, so CI behaves like
# the laptop and VIP mu-plugins are loaded in PHPUnit (no stubs needed).
name: CI

on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  checks:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 22
          cache: npm

      - name: Start WordPress, install dependencies, build
        run: make setup

      # Pyramid order: cheapest checks first, so failures come back fast.
      - name: Static analysis (PHPCS + PHPStan + ESLint + tsc)
        run: make lint

      - name: Unit tests (Jest)
        run: make test-js

      - name: Integration tests (PHPUnit + WP test suite)
        run: make test-php

      - name: Install Playwright browser
        run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium

      - name: End-to-end tests (Playwright)
        run: make test-e2e

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: failure()
        with:
          name: playwright-traces
          path: artifacts/

The gates earned their keep: the first CI run failed at PHPCS with several hundred findings across about 50 files. PHPCBF fixed most automatically, and the last 10 were fixed by hand. One project decision: phpcs.xml.dist requires short arrays [ ] (WordPress-Extra bans them by default), as most modern WordPress and VIP code does. Where a VIP rule is knowingly broken, the code says why on the same line (e.g. the 60s AI timeout in Q35).

How it's tested: composer lint (PHPCS) is clean, npm run lint:js is clean, and npm run typecheck passes.

✓ PHPCS (WordPress-Extra + VIP-Go) clean✓ ESLint clean✓ tsc --noEmit passes

Q10.How do you set up PHPUnit with the WordPress test suite for a plugin?

"wp scaffold plugin-tests, the Composer dev dependencies, a separate test database, a bootstrap that loads my plugin early, and tests that extend WP_UnitTestCase."

Picture thisA flight simulator. You need a separate copy of WordPress and a throwaway database that resets after every test, so you can crash as often as you like without touching real data.
  1. Scaffold: wp scaffold plugin-tests md-blocks creates phpunit.xml.dist, tests/bootstrap.php, a sample test and bin/install-wp-tests.sh.
  2. Composer dev deps: phpunit/phpunit, yoast/phpunit-polyfills (the core test library needs it), and optionally wp-phpunit/wp-phpunit so the test library comes from Composer instead of SVN.
  3. WordPress + test DB: from the install script, or simply wp-env, which has a tests environment built in.
  4. Bootstrap order: polyfills, then the test library's functions.php, then hook your plugin on muplugins_loaded, then the WP test bootstrap.
  5. Write tests: extend WP_UnitTestCase, use set_up()/tear_down() (snake case), and create data with self::factory().
// tests/bootstrap.php (core of it)
require dirname( __DIR__ ) . '/vendor/yoast/phpunit-polyfills/phpunitpolyfills-autoload.php';
$tests_dir = getenv( 'WP_TESTS_DIR' ) ?: '/tmp/wordpress-tests-lib';
require_once $tests_dir . '/includes/functions.php';
tests_add_filter( 'muplugins_loaded', fn() => require dirname( __DIR__ ) . '/md-blocks.php' );
require $tests_dir . '/includes/bootstrap.php';
Remember it as"Scaffold, Compose, Database, Bootstrap, Extend." Bootstrap order is "polyfill, functions, my plugin, boot".
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/tests/phpunit/, phpunit.xml.dist, local/public/wp-tests-config.php, plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q10-phpunit-setup/

This repo uses the Composer route: wp-phpunit/wp-phpunit provides the core test library, the Yoast polyfills load through the autoloader, and the VIP mu-plugins load the plugin. The tests prove the "flight simulator" claim: a separate tests_ table prefix, and posts created in one test are gone in the next.

bootstrap.php: polyfill, functions, my plugin, boot plugins/news-uk-practice/tests/phpunit/bootstrap.php
<?php
/**
 * Q10: PHPUnit bootstrap for the WordPress test suite.
 *
 * Order matters: "polyfill, functions, my plugin, boot".
 * - wp-phpunit (Composer) provides the core test library, so no SVN checkout.
 * - The Yoast polyfills are loaded by wp-tests-config.php via Composer's autoloader.
 * - The plugin is loaded the VIP way: VIP mu-plugins load client-mu-plugins,
 *   which call wpcom_vip_load_plugin( 'news-uk-practice' ). With a plain
 *   (non-VIP) plugin you would add instead:
 *     tests_add_filter( 'muplugins_loaded', fn() => require dirname( __DIR__, 2 ) . '/news-uk-practice.php' );
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

// Core test helpers first (tests_add_filter() and friends).
require_once getenv( 'WP_PHPUNIT__DIR' ) . '/includes/functions.php';

/*
 * Q19: if the VIP mu-plugins are not loaded (e.g. a CI job without them),
 * stub the VIP helpers the plugin calls, so platform code is still testable.
 * Here they ARE loaded, so these stubs only kick in elsewhere.
 */
tests_add_filter(
	'muplugins_loaded',
	function () {
		if ( ! function_exists( 'wpcom_vip_url_to_postid' ) ) {
			/**
			 * Stub: same signature, uncached core behaviour.
			 *
			 * @param string $url URL.
			 * @return int
			 */
			function wpcom_vip_url_to_postid( $url ) {
				return url_to_postid( $url ); // phpcs:ignore WordPressVIPMinimum.Functions.RestrictedFunctions.url_to_postid_url_to_postid
			}
		}
	},
	PHP_INT_MAX
);

require getenv( 'WP_PHPUNIT__DIR' ) . '/includes/bootstrap.php';
phpunit.xml.dist: finds *-test.php in every question folder phpunit.xml.dist

<?php
/**
 * PHPUnit config, used by wp-phpunit inside the WordPress container.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

if ( ! defined( 'ABSPATH' ) ) {
	define( 'ABSPATH', __DIR__ . '/wp/' );
}

require_once __DIR__ . '/wp-content/plugins/vendor/autoload.php';

define( 'DB_NAME', 'wordpress' );
define( 'DB_USER', 'wordpress' );
define( 'DB_PASSWORD', 'wordpress' );
define( 'DB_HOST', 'db' );
define( 'DB_CHARSET', 'utf8' );
define( 'DB_COLLATE', '' );

define( 'WP_DEBUG', true );
define( 'JETPACK_DEV_DEBUG', true ); // phpcs:ignore WordPressVIPMinimum.Constants.RestrictedConstants.DefiningRestrictedConstant
define( 'VIP_GO_APP_ENVIRONMENT', false );
define( 'FILES_CLIENT_SITE_ID', 123456 );
define( 'VIP_ENABLE_VIP_SEARCH', false );

define( 'WP_CONTENT_DIR', __DIR__ . '/wp-content' );
define( 'WPMU_PLUGIN_DIR', WP_CONTENT_DIR . '/mu-plugins' );

if ( file_exists( WPMU_PLUGIN_DIR . '/000-pre-vip-config/requires.php' ) ) {
	require_once WPMU_PLUGIN_DIR . '/000-pre-vip-config/requires.php';
}

if ( file_exists( __DIR__ . '/vip-config/vip-config.php' ) ) {
	require __DIR__ . '/vip-config/vip-config.php';
}

// Same object cache server as the site (wp-config.php). Without this the VIP
// drop-in tries 127.0.0.1, fails quietly, and wp_cache_add()/incr() return false.
// Set via $GLOBALS: PHPUnit includes this file from inside a function, so a
// plain $memcached_servers = ... would be a local variable the cache never sees.
$GLOBALS['memcached_servers'] = [ 'default' => [ 'memcached:11211' ] ]; // phpcs:ignore WordPress.WP.GlobalVariablesOverride.Prohibited

// Keep test cache entries apart from the site's: same server, different keys.
// Without a salt, tests (blog 1, tests_ tables) overwrite the site's cached
// users, options and posts.
define( 'WP_CACHE_KEY_SALT', 'news_uk_tests_' );

$table_prefix = 'tests_'; // phpcs:ignore WordPress.WP.GlobalVariablesOverride.Prohibited

define( 'WP_TESTS_DOMAIN', 'example.org' );
define( 'WP_TESTS_EMAIL', 'admin@example.org' );
define( 'WP_TESTS_TITLE', 'Test Blog' );
define( 'WP_PHP_BINARY', 'php' );
class-setup-test.php: proves isolation and reset plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q10-phpunit-setup/tests/class-setup-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q10: prove the test environment is set up the way the answer describes.
 *
 * "Flight simulator": a real WordPress, a throwaway database, reset after
 * every test. Test names are numbered so they run in order.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Setup tests.
 */
class Setup_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Shared fixture: created once for the whole class, still rolled back after.
	 *
	 * @var int
	 */
	private static $editor_id;

	/**
	 * Runs once before the class. Use the factory passed in.
	 *
	 * @param \WP_UnitTest_Factory $factory Factory.
	 */
	public static function wpSetUpBeforeClass( $factory ) {
		self::$editor_id = $factory->user->create( [ 'role' => 'editor' ] );
	}

	/**
	 * Separate test DB: table prefix from wp-tests-config.php, never the site's.
	 */
	public function test_1_uses_the_separate_test_tables() {
		global $wpdb;

		$this->assertSame( 'tests_', $wpdb->prefix );
	}

	/**
	 * The plugin is loaded before tests run (via client-mu-plugins).
	 */
	public function test_2_plugin_is_loaded() {
		$this->assertTrue( function_exists( 'News_UK_Practice\register_block' ) );
		$this->assertTrue( did_action( 'init' ) > 0 );
	}

	/**
	 * Create data freely: it is rolled back after this test.
	 */
	public function test_3_factory_creates_posts() {
		self::factory()->post->create_many( 3, [ 'post_title' => 'Throwaway' ] );

		$this->assertCount( 3, get_posts( [ 's' => 'Throwaway' ] ) );
	}

	/**
	 * ...and the previous test's posts are gone: each test starts clean.
	 */
	public function test_4_database_was_reset() {
		$this->assertCount( 0, get_posts( [ 's' => 'Throwaway' ] ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Logged-in context is just a function call.
	 */
	public function test_5_can_act_as_a_user() {
		wp_set_current_user( self::$editor_id );

		$this->assertTrue( current_user_can( 'edit_others_posts' ) );
	}
}

Setup gotchas found on the first real run (each is a good "how do you set up PHPUnit" story):

  • PHPUnit includes the bootstrap from inside a function. So a plain $memcached_servers = ... in wp-tests-config.php is a local variable: the object cache silently used 127.0.0.1 and every wp_cache_add() failed. Fix: $GLOBALS['memcached_servers'].
  • Once tests and the site share Memcached, give tests their own WP_CACHE_KEY_SALT, or test data overwrites the site's cached users and options.
  • WP_UnitTestCase unregisters all meta keys after every test, so tests that need registered meta re-register it in set_up() (see Q4).
  • Data providers run before the DB transaction, so create posts in the test, not in the provider (see Q11).

How it's tested: Run: docker compose exec -w /var/www/html/wp-content wordpress phpunit. Result: 136 tests, 296 assertions, all passing.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q11.Write a PHPUnit test for a dynamic block. What would you test?

"I check that it's registered, that the happy path renders, that bad input renders nothing, and that output is escaped. I create real posts with factories and render through do_blocks()."

Picture thisTesting a vending machine: does it exist (registered)? Put in the right coin, do you get the snack (happy path)? Put in a button or nothing, does it refuse politely (edge cases)? Can someone poison it (escaping)?

Checklist: registered; renders for valid attributes; empty for invalid, missing or draft posts; output escaped; supports add wrapper classes; cache is invalidated when the post changes.

class Test_Article_Teaser_Block extends WP_UnitTestCase {

    private function render( $post_id ) {
        return do_blocks( sprintf( '<!-- wp:md/article-teaser {"postId":%d} /-->', $post_id ) );
    }

    public function test_block_is_registered() {
        $this->assertTrue( WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->is_registered( 'md/article-teaser' ) );
    }

    public function test_renders_escaped_linked_title() {
        $id   = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_title' => '<script>x</script>Hello Times' ] );
        $html = $this->render( $id );
        $this->assertStringContainsString( get_permalink( $id ), $html );
        $this->assertStringNotContainsString( '<script>', $html );
    }

    /** @dataProvider bad_posts */
    public function test_renders_nothing_for_bad_input( $make_id ) {
        $this->assertSame( '', trim( $this->render( $make_id( $this ) ) ) );
    }

    public static function bad_posts() {
        return [
            'draft'   => [ fn( $t ) => $t::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_status' => 'draft' ] ) ],
            'missing' => [ fn() => 999999 ],
            'zero'    => [ fn() => 0 ],
        ];
    }
}
Remember it as"Exists, Works, Refuses, Safe." And say it out loud: "the data provider means one test covers every edge case."
Your storyThis is where your PHP depth shows. Practise typing this from memory in an empty environment until it takes under 5 minutes.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q11-dynamic-block-test/

The full checklist for the Article Teaser: Exists (registered and dynamic), Works (link, and the title stays live after an edit), Refuses (one data-provider test for draft, private, trashed, missing, zero and no ID), Safe (escaped title), plus supports classes and the image toggle.

class-article-teaser-test.php plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q11-dynamic-block-test/tests/class-article-teaser-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q11: PHPUnit tests for the dynamic Article Teaser block (q01).
 *
 * Checklist: Exists, Works, Refuses, Safe.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Article Teaser tests.
 */
class Article_Teaser_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Render the block the way WordPress does on the front end.
	 *
	 * @param array $attributes Block attributes.
	 * @return string
	 */
	private function render( array $attributes ) {
		// Cast to object: an empty PHP array encodes as [] (not a valid block
		// comment), an empty object as {}.
		return do_blocks( '<!-- wp:news-uk/article-teaser ' . wp_json_encode( (object) $attributes ) . ' /-->' );
	}

	/**
	 * Exists: registered from block.json, with its attributes.
	 */
	public function test_block_is_registered() {
		$block = \WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( 'news-uk/article-teaser' );

		$this->assertNotNull( $block );
		$this->assertArrayHasKey( 'postId', $block->attributes );
		$this->assertTrue( $block->is_dynamic() );
	}

	/**
	 * Works: a published post renders as a link with the current title.
	 */
	public function test_renders_link_to_published_post() {
		$post_id = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_title' => 'Budget day live' ] );

		$html = $this->render( [ 'postId' => $post_id ] );

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'href="' . get_permalink( $post_id ) . '"', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'Budget day live', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * Works: the title is live, not frozen at save time.
	 */
	public function test_shows_updated_title_without_resaving() {
		$post_id = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_title' => 'Old headline' ] );
		wp_update_post(
			[
				'ID'         => $post_id,
				'post_title' => 'New headline',
			]
		);

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'New headline', $this->render( [ 'postId' => $post_id ] ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Refuses: anything that is not a published post renders nothing.
	 *
	 * Data providers run before the test's DB transaction starts, so they pass
	 * plain values and the test creates the posts (otherwise they would leak).
	 *
	 * @dataProvider bad_posts
	 *
	 * @param string|int|null $input A post status to create, or a raw postId.
	 */
	public function test_renders_nothing_for_bad_input( $input ) {
		$attributes = [];
		if ( is_string( $input ) ) {
			$attributes['postId'] = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_status' => $input ] );
		} elseif ( null !== $input ) {
			$attributes['postId'] = $input;
		}

		$this->assertSame( '', trim( $this->render( $attributes ) ) );
	}

	/**
	 * One test, every edge case.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function bad_posts() {
		return [
			'draft'      => [ 'draft' ],
			'private'    => [ 'private' ],
			'trashed'    => [ 'trash' ],
			'missing ID' => [ 999999 ],
			'zero'       => [ 0 ],
			'no ID'      => [ null ],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Safe: HTML in a title is escaped, not executed.
	 */
	public function test_escapes_title() {
		// WordPress's kses filter would strip <script> on save, so the render
		// code would never see it. Turn kses off to simulate bad data that got in
		// another way (an import, direct DB write): escaping must still hold.
		kses_remove_filters();
		$post_id = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_title' => 'Budget <script>alert(1)</script>' ] );
		kses_init_filters();

		$html = $this->render( [ 'postId' => $post_id ] );

		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( '<script>', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( '&lt;script&gt;', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * Supports: block supports add their classes/styles to the wrapper.
	 */
	public function test_supports_add_wrapper_classes_and_styles() {
		$post_id = self::factory()->post->create();

		$html = $this->render(
			[
				'postId'          => $post_id,
				'backgroundColor' => 'accent',
				'style'           => [ 'spacing' => [ 'padding' => [ 'top' => '20px' ] ] ],
			]
		);

		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'wp-block-news-uk-article-teaser', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'has-accent-background-color', $html );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'padding-top:20px', $html );
	}

	/**
	 * The showImage toggle adds the featured image only when asked.
	 */
	public function test_show_image_toggle() {
		$post_id = self::factory()->post->create();
		$image   = self::factory()->attachment->create_upload_object( DIR_TESTDATA . '/images/canola.jpg', $post_id );
		set_post_thumbnail( $post_id, $image );

		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( '<img', $this->render( [ 'postId' => $post_id ] ) );
		$this->assertStringContainsString(
			'<img',
			$this->render(
				[
					'postId'    => $post_id,
					'showImage' => true,
				]
			)
		);
	}
}

Two bugs the first run caught, both in the test itself: wp_json_encode( [] ) gives [], which isn't a valid block comment, so the test casts to an object to get {}. And WordPress's kses filter strips <script> from titles on save, so the escaping code was never reached. The test turns kses off to simulate bad data that got in some other way (e.g. an import), and proves escaping still holds.

How it's tested: All pass, including each data-provider case. Practise typing the data-provider test from memory.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q12.Do you know the testing pyramid method?

"Lots of fast unit tests at the bottom, fewer integration tests in the middle, a handful of end-to-end tests on top, all sitting on static analysis."

Picture thisChecking a car. Unit tests are checking each bolt and spark plug on the bench: cheap, fast, thousands of them. Integration tests check the engine runs once assembled. E2E is a test drive: realistic but slow and expensive, so you only do a few. You wouldn't find a loose bolt by test-driving 500 times.
            /\        E2E (Playwright)                 few, slow, realistic
           /  \
          /----\      Integration (WP_UnitTestCase,    some
         /      \     REST, block rendering)
        /--------\
       /          \   Unit (PHPUnit, Jest)             many, fast
      /------------\
      Static analysis (PHPCS, PHPStan, ESLint)         foundation

Why this shape: going up, tests get slower, more expensive and flakier. At the bottom you get feedback in seconds.

Anti-pattern: the "ice cream cone", the pyramid upside down: mostly manual or E2E testing and hardly any unit tests. Slow, flaky, and bugs are found late.

Remember it as"Many small, few big." Upside down it's an ice cream cone, and that melts.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q12-testing-pyramid/

The same feature tested at each layer. Unit: reading_time_minutes() with a plain TestCase (no DB, milliseconds). Integration: the byline bindings source in Q4 with real users and posts. E2E: the Playwright specs. make test and CI run them in pyramid order, cheapest first.

Unit test: plain TestCase, many cases plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q12-testing-pyramid/tests/class-reading-time-unit-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q12, bottom of the pyramid: a UNIT test. Plain TestCase, no database, no
 * factories, no hooks. Runs in milliseconds, so we can afford many cases.
 *
 * Compare with test_custom_byline_source() in q04 (INTEGRATION: real users,
 * posts and block rendering) and the Playwright specs (E2E: real browser).
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use Yoast\PHPUnitPolyfills\TestCases\TestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q04\reading_time_minutes;

/**
 * Reading time unit tests.
 */
class Reading_Time_Unit_Test extends TestCase {

	/**
	 * Minutes are rounded up and never zero.
	 *
	 * @dataProvider cases
	 *
	 * @param string $content  Content.
	 * @param int    $expected Minutes.
	 */
	public function test_reading_time( $content, $expected ) {
		$this->assertSame( $expected, reading_time_minutes( $content ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Cases.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function cases() {
		return [
			'empty is still 1 minute' => [ '', 1 ],
			'200 words is 1 minute'   => [ str_repeat( 'word ', 200 ), 1 ],
			'201 words rounds up'     => [ str_repeat( 'word ', 201 ), 2 ],
			'HTML is not counted'     => [ '<p class="a b c d e">' . str_repeat( 'word ', 200 ) . '</p>', 1 ],
			'block comments ignored'  => [ '<!-- wp:paragraph {"a":"b c d"} -->' . str_repeat( 'word ', 400 ) . '<!-- /wp:paragraph -->', 2 ],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Custom speed is respected, and a bad speed cannot divide by zero.
	 */
	public function test_words_per_minute() {
		$this->assertSame( 4, reading_time_minutes( str_repeat( 'word ', 400 ), 100 ) );
		$this->assertSame( 400, reading_time_minutes( str_repeat( 'word ', 400 ), 0 ) );
	}
}
Makefile: test runs lint, then Jest, then PHPUnit, then Playwright Makefile
# Pyramid order: cheapest and fastest first.
test: lint test-js test-php test-e2e

How it's tested: Repo totals: static analysis (PHPCS, ESLint, tsc), 32 Jest tests, 136 PHPUnit tests (296 assertions), 11 Playwright tests. Many small tests, few big ones.

Q13.How do you decide when to use PHPUnit and when to use Jest in Gutenberg block development?

"Test code in the language it runs in, at the layer where the logic lives. PHP code gets PHPUnit, JS code gets Jest."

Jest + React Testing Library (JS)PHPUnit (PHP)
Edit component and its controls
save output (static blocks)
Utility functions
Data store selectors
Transforms and deprecations		Block registration
render.php output (dynamic blocks)
Attribute sanitisation
REST endpoints the block calls
Meta registration, bindings sources, hooks
Example"My Article Teaser is dynamic, so most of its logic is in render.php: mostly PHPUnit, plus a small Jest suite for the post picker in the sidebar. A static callout block is the opposite: mostly Jest, because save() is JavaScript."
Remember it as"Where does the logic live? Test it there."
