📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q13-phpunit-or-jest/

A static Callout block: its logic is in JS save(), so it's tested with Jest. Compare Q11, where the dynamic teaser's logic is in render.php and is tested with PHPUnit.

settings.js: save() is the logic plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q13-phpunit-or-jest/settings.js
// Static block: this markup is stored in post_content. That makes it JS logic, so Jest tests it.
function save( { attributes } ) {
	return (
		<div
			{ ...useBlockProps.save( {
				className: className( attributes.tone ),
				// Warnings are announced by screen readers; info callouts are not.
				// undefined = attribute left out entirely.
				role: attributes.tone === 'warning' ? 'alert' : undefined,
			} ) }
		>
			{ /* RichText.Content prints the saved rich text (with its bold/links) as HTML. */ }
			<RichText.Content tagName="p" value={ attributes.text } />
		</div>
	);
}

export const settings = { ...metadata, edit: Edit, save };
Jest: test save() output and round-trip plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q13-phpunit-or-jest/test/save.test.js
/**
 * Q13: Jest tests for a static block's save().
 *
 * getSaveContent() runs save() exactly as the editor does when a post is saved,
 * and returns the HTML string, so the tests assert on real output.
 */

/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
import {
	registerBlockType,
	unregisterBlockType,
	createBlock,
	getSaveContent,
	serialize,
	parse,
} from '@wordpress/blocks';

/**
 * Internal dependencies
 */
import { settings } from '../settings';

describe( 'Callout save()', () => {
	beforeAll( () => registerBlockType( settings.name, settings ) );
	afterAll( () => unregisterBlockType( settings.name ) );

	it( 'saves an info callout without an alert role', () => {
		const html = getSaveContent( settings.name, {
			tone: 'info',
			text: 'Polls close at 10pm.',
		} );

		expect( html ).toBe(
			'<div class="news-uk-callout news-uk-callout--info"><p>Polls close at 10pm.</p></div>'
		);
	} );

	it( 'marks a warning callout as an alert for screen readers', () => {
		const html = getSaveContent( settings.name, {
			tone: 'warning',
			text: 'Road closed.',
		} );

		expect( html ).toContain( 'news-uk-callout--warning' );
		expect( html ).toContain( 'role="alert"' );
	} );

	// The most valuable static-block test: what we save must parse back as valid,
	// otherwise editors see "invalid content" the next time they open the post.
	it( 'round-trips: saved markup parses back as a valid block', () => {
		const block = createBlock( settings.name, {
			tone: 'warning',
			text: 'Road <strong>closed</strong>.',
		} );

		const [ parsed ] = parse( serialize( block ) );

		expect( parsed.isValid ).toBe( true );
		expect( parsed.attributes.text.toString() ).toBe(
			'Road <strong>closed</strong>.'
		);
	} );
} );

How it's tested: 3 passing. Gotcha found while building this: npm had installed two copies of @wordpress/element, which made RichText.Content render an extra <div> in Jest. Adding @wordpress/element as a direct dependency deduped it.

✓ Jest passes

Q14.How do you deal with API requests, responses, authentication and error handling?

"Correct status codes and one consistent error shape, the right auth for each type of client, a real permission check on every route, input validated by schema, and outbound calls that time out and fall back to cached data."

Picture thisA nightclub door. Authentication is checking your ID (who are you?). Authorisation is the guest list (are you allowed in this room?). Validation is "no glass bottles": bad input is turned away at the door, not dealt with inside.
  • Responses: 200 OK, 201 Created, 400 bad input, 401 not logged in, 403 not allowed, 404 not found, 500 our fault. Errors always come back as a WP_Error (code, message, data.status).
  • Authentication:
    • Logged-in JS in WP: cookie + X-WP-Nonce (the wp_rest nonce).
    • Server-to-server: Application Passwords over HTTPS.
    • OAuth or JWT if the project needs them. Never admin credentials for scripts.
  • Authorisation: permission_callback with current_user_can( 'edit_post', $id ). Never __return_true on anything that writes or exposes private data.
  • Validation: declare the schema in args so bad input is rejected before your code runs.
  • Outbound calls: set a timeout, check is_wp_error() and the response code, log the failure (don't show it to the user), fall back to cached data.
register_rest_route( 'md/v1', '/teaser/(?P<id>\d+)', [
  'methods'             => 'POST',
  'args'                => [ 'id' => [ 'type' => 'integer', 'required' => true ] ],
  'permission_callback' => fn( $r ) => current_user_can( 'edit_post', $r['id'] ),
  'callback'            => 'md_update_teaser',
] );
Remember it as"Who are you, are you allowed, is it valid, and what if it fails?"
Your storyOAuth, Salesforce and Dynamics 365 integrations, and the CHOICE subscription renewal retry logic as a real example of handling failures.

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q14-rest-api/

/news-uk/v1/standfirst/{id}. Public GET for published posts, and POST that needs edit_post. The route uses schema validation, returns 401 vs 403 correctly, and gives drafts the same 404 as missing posts so it doesn't leak that they exist.

register_rest_route with args schema plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q14-rest-api/load.php
function register_routes() {
	register_rest_route(
		NAMESPACE_V1,
		'/standfirst/(?P<id>\d+)',
		[
			[
				'methods'             => \WP_REST_Server::READABLE,
				'callback'            => __NAMESPACE__ . '\get_standfirst',
				'permission_callback' => '__return_true', // Read-only, published data only (checked in callback).
				'args'                => [ 'id' => id_arg() ],
			],
			[
				'methods'             => \WP_REST_Server::EDITABLE,
				'callback'            => __NAMESPACE__ . '\update_standfirst',
				'permission_callback' => __NAMESPACE__ . '\can_edit',
				'args'                => [
					'id'         => id_arg(),
					'standfirst' => [
						'type'              => 'string',
						'required'          => true,
						'maxLength'         => 300,
						// Gotcha: a custom sanitize_callback replaces WordPress's default
						// (rest_sanitize_request_arg), and that default is what runs the
						// schema checks. So declare validation explicitly, or type and
						// maxLength are silently ignored.
						'validate_callback' => 'rest_validate_request_arg',
						'sanitize_callback' => 'sanitize_text_field',
					],
				],
			],
			'schema' => __NAMESPACE__ . '\schema',
		]
	);
}
add_action( 'rest_api_init', __NAMESPACE__ . '\register_routes' );
permission_callback: 401 vs 403 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q14-rest-api/load.php
function can_edit( \WP_REST_Request $request ) {
	if ( ! is_user_logged_in() ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'rest_not_logged_in', __( 'You must be logged in.', 'news-uk' ), [ 'status' => 401 ] );
	}

	if ( ! current_user_can( 'edit_post', (int) $request['id'] ) ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'rest_forbidden', __( 'You cannot edit this post.', 'news-uk' ), [ 'status' => 403 ] );
	}

	return true;
PHPUnit: one test per status code plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q14-rest-api/tests/class-rest-api-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q14: one test per status code. rest_do_request() runs the real REST server
 * (routing, auth, schema validation) without HTTP.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_REST_Request;
use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * REST API tests.
 */
class Rest_Api_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Published post.
	 *
	 * @var int
	 */
	private static $post_id;

	/**
	 * Users.
	 *
	 * @var int[]
	 */
	private static $users = [];

	/**
	 * Shared fixtures.
	 *
	 * @param \WP_UnitTest_Factory $factory Factory.
	 */
	public static function wpSetUpBeforeClass( $factory ) {
		self::$post_id = $factory->post->create();
		update_post_meta( self::$post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', 'Original' );
		self::$users['editor']     = $factory->user->create( [ 'role' => 'editor' ] );
		self::$users['subscriber'] = $factory->user->create( [ 'role' => 'subscriber' ] );
	}

	/**
	 * Send a request.
	 *
	 * @param string $method HTTP method.
	 * @param int    $id     Post ID.
	 * @param array  $body   Body params.
	 * @return \WP_REST_Response
	 */
	private function request( $method, $id, $body = [] ) {
		$request = new WP_REST_Request( $method, '/news-uk/v1/standfirst/' . $id );
		$request->set_body_params( $body );
		return rest_do_request( $request );
	}

	/**
	 * 200: anyone can read a published post's standfirst.
	 */
	public function test_get_200() {
		$response = $this->request( 'GET', self::$post_id );

		$this->assertSame( 200, $response->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'Original', $response->get_data()['standfirst'] );
	}

	/**
	 * 404: drafts look exactly like missing posts.
	 */
	public function test_get_404_for_draft_and_missing() {
		$draft = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_status' => 'draft' ] );

		$this->assertSame( 404, $this->request( 'GET', $draft )->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 404, $this->request( 'GET', 999999 )->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'rest_post_not_found', $this->request( 'GET', 999999 )->get_data()['code'] );
	}

	/**
	 * 401: not logged in.
	 */
	public function test_post_401_when_logged_out() {
		$this->assertSame( 401, $this->request( 'POST', self::$post_id, [ 'standfirst' => 'x' ] )->get_status() );
	}

	/**
	 * 403: logged in, but not allowed.
	 */
	public function test_post_403_for_subscriber() {
		wp_set_current_user( self::$users['subscriber'] );

		$this->assertSame( 403, $this->request( 'POST', self::$post_id, [ 'standfirst' => 'x' ] )->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'Original', get_post_meta( self::$post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
	}

	/**
	 * 400: schema rejects bad input before our callback runs.
	 *
	 * @dataProvider bad_bodies
	 *
	 * @param array $body Body.
	 */
	public function test_post_400_for_invalid_input( $body ) {
		wp_set_current_user( self::$users['editor'] );

		$response = $this->request( 'POST', self::$post_id, $body );

		$this->assertSame( 400, $response->get_status() );
		$this->assertContains( $response->get_data()['code'], [ 'rest_invalid_param', 'rest_missing_callback_param' ] );
	}

	/**
	 * Invalid bodies.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function bad_bodies() {
		return [
			'missing'  => [ [] ],
			'too long' => [ [ 'standfirst' => str_repeat( 'a', 301 ) ] ],
			'array'    => [ [ 'standfirst' => [ 'a' ] ] ],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * 200: an editor can update, and input is sanitised.
	 */
	public function test_post_200_for_editor_and_sanitises() {
		wp_set_current_user( self::$users['editor'] );

		$response = $this->request( 'POST', self::$post_id, [ 'standfirst' => '<b>Rates</b> held' ] );

		$this->assertSame( 200, $response->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'Rates held', $response->get_data()['standfirst'] );
	}
}

Bug the test caught: a 301-character or array standfirst was accepted (200). A custom sanitize_callback replaces WordPress's default (rest_parse_request_arg, which validates against the schema and then sanitises), and that default is what runs the schema checks, so type and maxLength were silently ignored. Fix: also declare 'validate_callback' => 'rest_validate_request_arg'. Worth saying in the interview.

How it's tested: rest_do_request() exercises routing, auth and schema validation without HTTP: 200, 404, 401, 403, 400 (three bad inputs) and 200 with sanitising. All pass.

✓ PHPUnit passes

3. WordPress VIP platform and performance

You're VIP certified and have shipped on VIP, which is a real advantage. Make every answer concrete with the Ziff Davis caching work.

Q15.Explain object caching.

"A persistent object cache keeps the results of expensive work in memory between requests, so the next request skips the work. On VIP it's Memcached."

Picture thisA sticky note on your monitor. The first time someone asks "what are the 10 most-read stories?", you run the slow database query and write the answer on a sticky note. Everyone after that just reads the note. When a story changes, you throw the note away (invalidate) and write a new one next time.
function md_most_read() {
    $ids = wp_cache_get( 'most_read', 'md' );           // 1. look at the note
    if ( false === $ids ) {                              // 2. no note (cache miss)
        $ids = md_expensive_most_read_query();           // 3. do the slow work
        wp_cache_set( 'most_read', $ids, 'md', 5 * MINUTE_IN_SECONDS ); // 4. write the note
    }
    return $ids;
}
add_action( 'save_post', fn() => wp_cache_delete( 'most_read', 'md' ) ); // 5. bin it on change
  • API: wp_cache_get/set/delete, with a group and an expiry. Core already caches posts, meta, terms and options this way.
  • VIP cautions: keep values small (about a 1 MB item limit), protect hot keys from a stampede, never call wp_cache_flush().
Remember it as"Get, Miss, Compute, Set, Invalidate."
Your storyZiff Davis: per-product cache invalidation and delta-based sync kept prices fresh without cold-starting the whole cache.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q15-object-cache/

The sticky note in code: get, miss, compute, set, invalidate. The tests prove it by counting DB queries: the second call makes zero.

load.php plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q15-object-cache/load.php
function most_read_ids() {
	$ids = wp_cache_get( KEY, GROUP );               // 1. Look at the sticky note.

	if ( false === $ids ) {                          // 2. No note: cache miss.
		$ids = get_posts(                            // 3. Do the slow work, once.
			[
				'post_type'        => 'post',
				'post_status'      => 'publish',
				'orderby'          => 'comment_count',
				'order'            => 'DESC',
				'posts_per_page'   => 10,
				'fields'           => 'ids',
				'no_found_rows'    => true,
				'suppress_filters' => false,
			]
		);
		wp_cache_set( KEY, $ids, GROUP, 5 * MINUTE_IN_SECONDS ); // 4. Write the note.
	}

	return $ids;
}

/**
 * 5. Bin the note when the answer may have changed.
 *
 * @param int $post_id Post ID.
 * @return void
 */
function invalidate( $post_id ) {
	if ( 'post' === get_post_type( $post_id ) ) {
		wp_cache_delete( KEY, GROUP );
	}
}
add_action( 'save_post', __NAMESPACE__ . '\invalidate' );
add_action( 'deleted_post', __NAMESPACE__ . '\invalidate' );
add_action( 'wp_update_comment_count', __NAMESPACE__ . '\invalidate' );
PHPUnit: query counts + invalidation plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q15-object-cache/tests/class-object-cache-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q15: prove the cache is used and invalidated, by counting DB queries.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q15\most_read_ids;

/**
 * Object cache tests.
 */
class Object_Cache_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Number of DB queries $callback makes.
	 *
	 * @param callable $callback Code to measure.
	 * @return int
	 */
	private function queries( callable $callback ) {
		global $wpdb;
		$before = $wpdb->num_queries;
		$callback();
		return $wpdb->num_queries - $before;
	}

	/**
	 * Start every test with an empty note.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		wp_cache_delete( 'most_read_ids', 'news_uk' );
	}

	/**
	 * Miss queries the DB; hit does not.
	 */
	public function test_second_call_is_served_from_cache() {
		$hot  = self::factory()->post->create();
		$cold = self::factory()->post->create();
		self::factory()->comment->create_post_comments( $hot, 3 );

		$this->assertGreaterThan( 0, $this->queries( fn() => most_read_ids() ), 'Miss hits the DB.' );
		$this->assertSame( 0, $this->queries( fn() => most_read_ids() ), 'Hit does not.' );
		$this->assertSame( [ $hot, $cold ], most_read_ids() );
	}

	/**
	 * Publishing a post throws the note away.
	 */
	public function test_saving_a_post_invalidates() {
		most_read_ids();
		$this->assertNotFalse( wp_cache_get( 'most_read_ids', 'news_uk' ) );

		self::factory()->post->create();

		$this->assertFalse( wp_cache_get( 'most_read_ids', 'news_uk' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Saving a page (not a post) keeps the cache warm.
	 */
	public function test_saving_a_page_keeps_cache() {
		most_read_ids();
		self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_type' => 'page' ] );

		$this->assertNotFalse( wp_cache_get( 'most_read_ids', 'news_uk' ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: This also checks that saving a page (not a post) leaves the cache warm. Runs against the real Memcached container.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q16.How do transients behave when an object cache is present, and when it is not?

"Without an object cache, transients are rows in wp_options. With one, they go straight into the cache and never touch the database. Either way they can disappear early, so always handle a miss."

Picture thisA transient is a parcel marked "use by Friday". With no object cache it goes into the filing cabinet (the database): it's durable, but it fills the cabinet. With an object cache it goes on the desk (memory): faster, but the desk gets cleared when it's full, so the parcel might be gone before Friday.
No persistent object cacheWith object cache (VIP)
Stored inTwo rows in wp_options: _transient_{name} and _transient_timeout_{name}Memcached, in the transient group (site-transient for site transients)
ExpiryDeleted when read after expiring, or by scheduled clean-upHandled by the cache. Can be evicted early under memory pressure
GotchaTransients with no expiry are autoloaded on every page. Heavy use bloats the options table and adds DB writesNothing in the DB at all
Remember it as"No cache: cabinet. Cache: desk. Either way: might be gone."
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q16-transients/

The test flips wp_using_ext_object_cache() and reads wp_options directly. Without an object cache there are two rows (_transient_ and _transient_timeout_). With one there are no rows, and the value sits in the transient cache group.

load.php: always handle the miss plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q16-transients/load.php
function trending_topics() {
	$topics = get_transient( 'news_uk_trending' );

	if ( false === $topics ) { // Expired, evicted, or never set: rebuild.
		$topics = fetch_trending_topics();
		set_transient( 'news_uk_trending', $topics, 10 * MINUTE_IN_SECONDS ); // Always set an expiry.
	}

	return $topics;
PHPUnit: cabinet vs desk plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q16-transients/tests/class-transients-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q16: show where a transient is stored with and without an object cache.
 * wp_using_ext_object_cache() switches between the two behaviours in-process.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q16\trending_topics;

/**
 * Transients tests.
 */
class Transients_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * What the site really uses, restored after each test.
	 *
	 * @var bool
	 */
	private $using_ext;

	/**
	 * Remember the real setting.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		$this->using_ext = wp_using_ext_object_cache();
	}

	/**
	 * Put it back.
	 */
	public function tear_down() {
		wp_using_ext_object_cache( $this->using_ext );
		parent::tear_down();
	}

	/**
	 * Read the raw row from wp_options, bypassing every cache.
	 *
	 * @param string $name Option name.
	 * @return string|null
	 */
	private function db_row( $name ) {
		global $wpdb;
		return $wpdb->get_var( $wpdb->prepare( "SELECT option_value FROM $wpdb->options WHERE option_name = %s", $name ) ); // phpcs:ignore WordPress.DB.DirectDatabaseQuery
	}

	/**
	 * No object cache: the filing cabinet (two option rows).
	 */
	public function test_without_object_cache_uses_options_table() {
		wp_using_ext_object_cache( false );

		set_transient( 'news_uk_demo', 'hello', HOUR_IN_SECONDS );

		$this->assertSame( 'hello', $this->db_row( '_transient_news_uk_demo' ) );
		$this->assertNotNull( $this->db_row( '_transient_timeout_news_uk_demo' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Object cache: the desk (memory). Nothing in the database at all.
	 */
	public function test_with_object_cache_skips_database() {
		wp_using_ext_object_cache( true );

		set_transient( 'news_uk_demo2', 'hello', HOUR_IN_SECONDS );

		$this->assertSame( 'hello', get_transient( 'news_uk_demo2' ) );
		$this->assertNull( $this->db_row( '_transient_news_uk_demo2' ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'hello', wp_cache_get( 'news_uk_demo2', 'transient' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Gone early (evicted)? The code rebuilds instead of breaking.
	 */
	public function test_handles_a_miss() {
		$calls = 0;
		add_filter(
			'news_uk_fetch_trending',
			function ( $topics ) use ( &$calls ) {
				++$calls;
				return $topics;
			}
		);

		trending_topics();
		trending_topics();
		$this->assertSame( 1, $calls, 'Second call served from the transient.' );

		delete_transient( 'news_uk_trending' ); // Simulate eviction.
		$this->assertContains( 'Budget', trending_topics() );
		$this->assertSame( 2, $calls );
	}
}

How it's tested: Three tests: options table without a cache, no DB row with a cache, and a rebuild after eviction.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q17.What do you know about Enterprise Search?

"VIP Enterprise Search is VIP's Elasticsearch service, built on ElasticPress. It gives better, faster site search and can take heavy WP_Query work off MySQL."

Picture thisMySQL looking for "all articles tagged Brexit with author X, sorted by popularity" is like reading every page of a book to find a word. Elasticsearch is the index at the back of the book: built in advance, so lookups are instant.
  • Indexes posts, terms and allow-listed meta.
  • Powers front-end search (better relevance and speed).
  • Offloads heavy queries (complex meta/tax queries, big sorts) by adding 'es' => true to WP_Query.
  • Things to watch: which meta keys are indexed (allow list), re-index with wp vip-search index, a short delay between save and searchable, monitor index health.
new WP_Query( [ 'tag' => 'brexit', 'meta_key' => 'views', 'orderby' => 'meta_value_num', 'es' => true ] );
Remember it as"Index at the back of the book; allow-list, re-index, slight delay."
Your storyElasticsearch is on your XWP stack. Be ready to say what you indexed and how you handled re-indexing.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q17-enterprise-search/

The meta allow list (vip_search_post_meta_allow_list) plus a heavy query that opts in with 'es' => true. Locally Enterprise Search is off, so the same query falls back to MySQL. That makes it safe to ship, and the fallback can be tested.

load.php plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q17-enterprise-search/load.php
function allow_list_meta( $allow ) {
	$allow[] = 'news_uk_views';
	$allow[] = 'news_uk_standfirst'; // Searchable standfirst.
	return $allow;
}
add_filter( 'vip_search_post_meta_allow_list', __NAMESPACE__ . '\allow_list_meta' );

/**
 * "Most viewed posts in a tag": a meta sort plus a tax query. Heavy on MySQL
 * at scale, cheap for Elasticsearch, so offload it with 'es' => true.
 *
 * @param string $tag Tag slug.
 * @return array WP_Query args.
 */
function most_viewed_in_tag_args( $tag ) {
	return [
		'es'             => true, // Offload to Elasticsearch when Enterprise Search is on.
		'tag'            => sanitize_title( $tag ),
		'meta_key'       => 'news_uk_views', // phpcs:ignore WordPress.DB.SlowDBQuery.slow_db_query_meta_key -- Answered by ES on VIP.
		'orderby'        => 'meta_value_num',
		'order'          => 'DESC',
		'posts_per_page' => 10,
		'no_found_rows'  => true,
	];
}
PHPUnit plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q17-enterprise-search/tests/class-enterprise-search-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q17: what can be tested without Elasticsearch: the allow list is wired, the
 * query asks for ES, and the MySQL fallback returns the same answer.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q17\most_viewed_in_tag;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q17\most_viewed_in_tag_args;

/**
 * Enterprise Search tests.
 */
class Enterprise_Search_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Meta used by the query is on the allow list, so ES indexes it.
	 */
	public function test_sort_meta_is_allow_listed() {
		$allow = apply_filters( 'vip_search_post_meta_allow_list', [] );

		$this->assertContains( most_viewed_in_tag_args( 'brexit' )['meta_key'], $allow );
	}

	/**
	 * The query opts in to Elasticsearch.
	 */
	public function test_query_opts_in_to_es() {
		$this->assertTrue( most_viewed_in_tag_args( 'brexit' )['es'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Without ES (locally), MySQL answers the same question correctly.
	 */
	public function test_mysql_fallback_orders_by_views() {
		$low  = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'tags_input' => [ 'brexit' ] ] );
		$high = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'tags_input' => [ 'brexit' ] ] );
		self::factory()->post->create( [ 'tags_input' => [ 'sport' ] ] );
		update_post_meta( $low, 'news_uk_views', 10 );
		update_post_meta( $high, 'news_uk_views', 500 );

		$this->assertSame( [ $high, $low ], wp_list_pluck( most_viewed_in_tag( 'brexit' ), 'ID' ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: Honest limit: with no Elasticsearch locally, the tests only cover wiring and the MySQL fallback. Use vip dev-env create --elasticsearch to try the real thing.

Q18.Describe your overall caching strategy and approach to performance optimization.

"Cache at every layer, make the queries that remain cheap, keep slow work out of the page request, and measure rather than guess. Any uncached expensive operation in a request is a bug."

Picture thisGetting a coffee. Fastest: you already have one in your hand (browser cache). Next: the kiosk at the station has it ready (CDN/edge). Next: the café has a pot brewed (object cache). Slowest: grind the beans from scratch (database query). Make sure almost nobody has to grind beans.

1. Layers: browser, then CDN/edge (whole pages), then object cache (fragments and query results), then efficient DB queries.

2. Cheap queries:

  • 'no_found_rows' => true when not paginating (skips counting every match)
  • Bounded posts_per_page, never -1
  • 'fields' => 'ids' when you only need IDs
  • Avoid post__not_in and unindexed meta queries at scale; never query_posts()

3. Use VIP's cached helpers instead of core's slow versions: wpcom_vip_url_to_postid(), wpcom_vip_attachment_url_to_postid(), wpcom_vip_get_page_by_path(), wpcom_vip_count_user_posts().

4. Keep work out of the request: no DB writes or schema changes on front-end GET requests. Remote calls get timeouts, are cached, and are refreshed by cron rather than on page load.

5. Platform rules: read-only file system: code can't write to its own folders. Uploads go to the VIP File System (through the normal upload APIs), and scratch files go in the temp directory. Use image resizing query args for responsive images.

6. Front end / Core Web Vitals (targets: LCP < 2.5s, CLS < 0.1, good INP): responsive images, lazy-load below the fold, fetchpriority="high" on the hero image, load block assets only when the block is on the page, minimal JS, reserve space for ads and embeds so the page doesn't jump.

7. Measure: Query Monitor, New Relic, Lighthouse, real-user data.

Remember it as"Layers, Lean queries, Later (cron), Look (measure)."
Your storyCache-aware rendering that kept pages up when the product API failed. That's exactly the reliability thinking a news publisher wants.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q18-performance/

Lean query args (IDs only, no_found_rows, a capped limit, no unused cache priming). A cache key that includes wp_cache_get_last_changed( 'posts' ), so it invalidates itself. A VIP helper with a core fallback. Per-block CSS loading, and resource hints.

Lean, cached query plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q18-performance/load.php
function latest_ids_in_category( $category_id, $limit = 5 ) {
	$key = 'latest_' . (int) $category_id . '_' . (int) $limit . '_' . wp_cache_get_last_changed( 'posts' );
	$ids = wp_cache_get( $key, 'news_uk' );

	if ( false === $ids ) {
		$ids = get_posts( latest_ids_args( $category_id, $limit ) );
		wp_cache_set( $key, $ids, 'news_uk', 5 * MINUTE_IN_SECONDS );
	}

	return $ids;
}

/**
 * The query args, separate so tests can check them.
 *
 * @param int $category_id Category.
 * @param int $limit       Max posts.
 * @return array
 */
function latest_ids_args( $category_id, $limit ) {
	return [
		'cat'                    => (int) $category_id,
		'posts_per_page'         => min( 50, max( 1, (int) $limit ) ), // Bounded, never -1.
		'fields'                 => 'ids',  // Only what we need.
		'no_found_rows'          => true,   // Skip SQL_CALC_FOUND_ROWS: we are not paginating.
		'update_post_term_cache' => false,  // Don't prime caches we won't read.
		'update_post_meta_cache' => false,
		'ignore_sticky_posts'    => true,
		'suppress_filters'       => false,
	];
}
PHPUnit: inspects the generated SQL plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q18-performance/tests/class-performance-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q18: lean queries and caching, checked by inspecting SQL and query counts.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q18\latest_ids_args;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q18\latest_ids_in_category;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q18\post_id_from_url;

/**
 * Performance tests.
 */
class Performance_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Query never asks for "all posts".
	 */
	public function test_limit_is_bounded() {
		$this->assertSame( 50, latest_ids_args( 1, 9999 )['posts_per_page'], 'Capped.' );
		$this->assertSame( 1, latest_ids_args( 1, -1 )['posts_per_page'], '-1 does not mean all.' );
	}

	/**
	 * The generated SQL selects only IDs and skips the found-rows count.
	 */
	public function test_sql_is_lean() {
		global $wpdb;
		$query = new \WP_Query( latest_ids_args( 1, 5 ) );

		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( 'SQL_CALC_FOUND_ROWS', $query->request );
		$this->assertMatchesRegularExpression( '/SELECT\s+' . preg_quote( $wpdb->posts, '/' ) . '\.ID\s+FROM/', $query->request );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'LIMIT 0, 5', $query->request );
	}

	/**
	 * Cached; a new post changes 'last_changed', so the cache key moves on its own.
	 */
	public function test_cached_and_self_invalidating() {
		global $wpdb;
		$cat = self::factory()->category->create();
		self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_category' => [ $cat ] ] );

		latest_ids_in_category( $cat );
		$before = $wpdb->num_queries;
		latest_ids_in_category( $cat );
		$this->assertSame( $before, $wpdb->num_queries, 'Second call is free.' );

		$new = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_category' => [ $cat ] ] );
		$this->assertContains( $new, latest_ids_in_category( $cat ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Uses the VIP helper (loaded via mu-plugins, or the bootstrap stub).
	 */
	public function test_post_id_from_url() {
		$id = self::factory()->post->create();

		$this->assertSame( $id, post_id_from_url( get_permalink( $id ) ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: The tests assert the SQL selects only ID with no SQL_CALC_FOUND_ROWS, that the second call is free, and that a new post appears without manual invalidation. Update: current VIP mu-plugins deprecate wpcom_vip_get_page_by_path(), because core's get_page_by_path() is cached since WordPress 6.1 (see Q24).

        run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium

      - name: End-to-end tests (Playwright)

        with:
          name: playwright-traces

4. React and JavaScript

News UK has asked specifically about Jest and React Testing Library. Practise both, and Playwright, before the interview.

Q20.On a scale of 1 to 5, how would you rate your React expertise?

"4 out of 5. I've written production React since 2018, rebuilt NewsCred's publishing experience in it, and I use React inside Gutenberg with @wordpress/data."

Picture thisA 5 is a dare: "go on, find what I don't know." A 4 with evidence says "I'm strong, I'm honest, and I know there's always more to learn." Interviewers trust the 4.

Be ready for these follow-ups (one line each):

  • useEffect dependencies: list everything the effect reads. A missing one means stale values. An object recreated every render means an infinite loop.
  • Keys in lists: use a stable unique id, not the array index, otherwise React mixes up items when the list is reordered.
  • Controlled inputs: the value comes from state and onChange updates it, so React is the single source of truth.
  • Lifting state: if two siblings need the same data, move it up to their common parent.
  • Avoiding re-renders: useMemo/useCallback/memo where it's measurable. In Gutenberg, make useSelect return small, stable values.
Your storyYou said 8/10 in screening. 4/5 is consistent with that. Keep the numbers aligned.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q20-react/

A "Related Links" block whose edit UI covers each follow-up question: useEffect deps + cleanup (the debounce hook), stable keys (post IDs, so reordering is safe), a controlled input (SearchControl), lifted state (both lists change postIds through the parent), and small, stable useSelect values.

edit.js: which line shows which React concept plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q20-react/edit.js
/**
 * Q20: the Related Links edit UI, a small React app inside a block.
 *
 * It's built to show each React follow-up question in real code:
 * - controlled input ............ the search box (search state below)
 * - useEffect deps + cleanup ..... use-debounced-value.js
 * - lifting state ................ add/remove/moveUp live here, children call them
 * - stable keys in lists ......... selected-list.js and search-results.js
 * - small, stable useSelect ...... titles below, results in search-results.js
 *
 * Component tree:
 *   Edit (owns postIds via attributes, and the search text)
 *   ├── SelectedList  (shows chosen posts; asks Edit to reorder/remove)
 *   ├── SearchControl (controlled input)
 *   └── SearchResults (queries posts; asks Edit to add one)
 */
import { useBlockProps } from '@wordpress/block-editor';
import { SearchControl } from '@wordpress/components';
import { useSelect } from '@wordpress/data';
import { store as coreStore } from '@wordpress/core-data';
// @wordpress/element is WordPress's wrapper around React: same hooks, and it
// uses the copy of React already loaded in the editor (no second React).
import { useState, useCallback } from '@wordpress/element';
import { __ } from '@wordpress/i18n';
import useDebouncedValue from './use-debounced-value';
import SearchResults from './search-results';
import SelectedList from './selected-list';

export default function Edit( { attributes, setAttributes } ) {
	// The chosen post IDs, in order. Stored in the block (block.json attribute),
	// so this is the single source of truth for both child lists.
	const { postIds } = attributes;

	// Controlled input: React state holds the value and the input only displays
	// it. Every keystroke goes through setSearch, so we can also clear it
	// programmatically (see add()).
	const [ search, setSearch ] = useState( '' );

	// The search term, but only once typing pauses for 300ms. Without this,
	// every keystroke would fire a REST request.
	const debouncedSearch = useDebouncedValue( search, 300 );

	// Titles for the chosen IDs. Keep useSelect results small and plain: here,
	// an array of strings. useSelect compares results shallowly, so when the
	// store changes for unrelated reasons (another block, a typing indicator...),
	// an array of the same strings does NOT re-render this component.
	// Returning whole post objects would re-render far more often.
	const titles = useSelect(
		( select ) =>
			postIds.map(
				( id ) =>
					select( coreStore ).getEntityRecord(
						'postType',
						'post',
						id
					)?.title?.rendered || `#${ id }` // Placeholder while loading.
			),
		[ postIds ] // Re-run the selector only when the list of IDs changes.
	);
	const items = postIds.map( ( id, i ) => ( { id, title: titles[ i ] } ) );

	// Lifted state: SelectedList and SearchResults both need to change postIds,
	// so the logic lives here in their common parent and they get callbacks.
	// They don't know about attributes at all: easy to reuse and to test.

	// useCallback keeps the same function between renders unless postIds
	// changes, so a memoised child wouldn't re-render needlessly.
	const add = useCallback(
		( id ) => {
			// Never mutate attributes: always pass a NEW array, otherwise React
			// and the block editor can't see that anything changed.
			setAttributes( { postIds: [ ...postIds, id ] } );
			setSearch( '' ); // Controlled input: clearing it is just setting state.
		},
		[ postIds, setAttributes ]
	);
	const remove = ( id ) =>
		setAttributes( { postIds: postIds.filter( ( x ) => x !== id ) } );
	const moveUp = ( index ) => {
		const next = [ ...postIds ]; // Copy, then swap two neighbours.
		[ next[ index - 1 ], next[ index ] ] = [
			next[ index ],
			next[ index - 1 ],
		];
		setAttributes( { postIds: next } );
	};

	return (
		// useBlockProps: the block's selectable wrapper (see q01 edit.js).
		<div { ...useBlockProps() }>
			<SelectedList
				items={ items }
				onMoveUp={ moveUp }
				onRemove={ remove }
			/>
			<SearchControl
				__nextHasNoMarginBottom
				label={ __( 'Search posts', 'news-uk' ) }
				value={ search }
				onChange={ setSearch }
			/>
			<SearchResults
				search={ debouncedSearch }
				excludeIds={ postIds }
				onSelect={ add }
			/>
		</div>
	);
}
use-debounced-value.js: deps + cleanup plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q20-react/use-debounced-value.js
import { useEffect, useState } from '@wordpress/element';

/**
 * Returns `value`, but only after it has stopped changing for `delay` ms.
 *
 * useEffect dependencies: everything the effect reads (value, delay).
 * Cleanup: cancel the pending timer when value changes or on unmount,
 * otherwise old timers fire with stale values.
 *
 * @param {*}      value Value to debounce.
 * @param {number} delay Milliseconds.
 * @return {*} Debounced value.
 */
export default function useDebouncedValue( value, delay = 300 ) {
	const [ debounced, setDebounced ] = useState( value );

	useEffect( () => {
		const timer = setTimeout( () => setDebounced( value ), delay );
		return () => clearTimeout( timer );
	}, [ value, delay ] );

	return debounced;
}
selected-list.js: keys by ID plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q20-react/selected-list.js
import { Button } from '@wordpress/components';
import { __, sprintf } from '@wordpress/i18n';

/**
 * Chosen posts, in order. Keys are post IDs, so reordering moves the right
 * DOM nodes (with the array index, React would mix up items' state).
 *
 * @param {Object}   props
 * @param {Object[]} props.items    [{ id, title }].
 * @param {Function} props.onMoveUp Called with an index.
 * @param {Function} props.onRemove Called with a post ID.
 */
export default function SelectedList( { items, onMoveUp, onRemove } ) {
	if ( ! items.length ) {
		return <p>{ __( 'No related links yet.', 'news-uk' ) }</p>;
	}

	return (
		<ol aria-label={ __( 'Related links', 'news-uk' ) }>
			{ items.map( ( item, index ) => (
				<li key={ item.id }>
					{ item.title }{ ' ' }
					<Button
						size="small"
						disabled={ index === 0 }
						label={ sprintf(
							/* translators: %s: post title */
							__( 'Move %s up', 'news-uk' ),
							item.title
						) }
						onClick={ () => onMoveUp( index ) }
					>
						↑
					</Button>
					<Button
						size="small"
						isDestructive
						label={ sprintf(
							/* translators: %s: post title */
							__( 'Remove %s', 'news-uk' ),
							item.title
						) }
						onClick={ () => onRemove( item.id ) }
					>
						×
					</Button>
				</li>
			) ) }
		</ol>
	);
}
Jest + RTL, fake timers for the debounce plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q20-react/test/edit.test.js
/**
 * External dependencies
 */
import { render, renderHook, screen, act } from '@testing-library/react';
import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';

/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
import { useSelect } from '@wordpress/data';

/**
 * Internal dependencies
 */
import Edit from '../edit';
import useDebouncedValue from '../use-debounced-value';

jest.mock( '@wordpress/block-editor', () => ( {
	useBlockProps: ( props = {} ) => props,
} ) );

jest.mock( '@wordpress/data', () => ( {
	...jest.requireActual( '@wordpress/data' ),
	useSelect: jest.fn(),
} ) );

const POSTS = {
	1: 'Budget day live',
	2: 'Election results',
	3: 'Weather warning',
};

// A fake store: getEntityRecord by ID, getEntityRecords by search term.
function fakeStore() {
	useSelect.mockImplementation( ( selector ) =>
		selector( () => ( {
			getEntityRecord: ( kind, name, id ) => ( {
				id,
				title: { rendered: POSTS[ id ] },
			} ),
			getEntityRecords: ( kind, name, { search } ) =>
				Object.entries( POSTS )
					.filter( ( [ , title ] ) =>
						title.toLowerCase().includes( search.toLowerCase() )
					)
					.map( ( [ id, title ] ) => ( {
						id: Number( id ),
						title: { rendered: title },
					} ) ),
		} ) )
	);
}

describe( 'useDebouncedValue', () => {
	beforeEach( () => jest.useFakeTimers() );
	afterEach( () => jest.useRealTimers() );

	it( 'only updates after the value stops changing', () => {
		const { result, rerender } = renderHook(
			( { value } ) => useDebouncedValue( value, 300 ),
			{ initialProps: { value: 'a' } }
		);

		rerender( { value: 'ab' } );
		act( () => jest.advanceTimersByTime( 200 ) );
		rerender( { value: 'abc' } ); // Cancels the 'ab' timer (cleanup).
		act( () => jest.advanceTimersByTime( 200 ) );
		expect( result.current ).toBe( 'a' );

		act( () => jest.advanceTimersByTime( 100 ) );
		expect( result.current ).toBe( 'abc' );
	} );
} );

describe( 'Related Links edit', () => {
	beforeEach( fakeStore );

	describe( 'searching (debounced, so fake timers)', () => {
		let user;
		beforeEach( () => {
			jest.useFakeTimers();
			user = userEvent.setup( {
				advanceTimers: jest.advanceTimersByTime,
			} );
		} );
		afterEach( () => jest.useRealTimers() );

		it( 'searches after typing and adds the chosen post', async () => {
			const setAttributes = jest.fn();
			render(
				<Edit
					attributes={ { postIds: [] } }
					setAttributes={ setAttributes }
				/>
			);

			await user.type( screen.getByRole( 'searchbox' ), 'elect' );
			act( () => jest.advanceTimersByTime( 300 ) );
			await user.click(
				screen.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Election results' } )
			);

			expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenCalledWith( { postIds: [ 2 ] } );
			expect( screen.getByRole( 'searchbox' ) ).toHaveValue( '' );
		} );

		it( 'hides posts that are already chosen from results', async () => {
			render(
				<Edit
					attributes={ { postIds: [ 2 ] } }
					setAttributes={ jest.fn() }
				/>
			);

			await user.type( screen.getByRole( 'searchbox' ), 'e' );
			act( () => jest.advanceTimersByTime( 300 ) );

			const results = screen.getByRole( 'list', {
				name: 'Search results',
			} );
			expect( results ).not.toHaveTextContent( 'Election results' );
			expect( results ).toHaveTextContent( 'Budget day live' );
		} );
	} );

	it( 'reorders and removes using post IDs', async () => {
		const setAttributes = jest.fn();
		render(
			<Edit
				attributes={ { postIds: [ 1, 2, 3 ] } }
				setAttributes={ setAttributes }
			/>
		);

		await userEvent.click(
			screen.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Move Weather warning up' } )
		);
		expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith( {
			postIds: [ 1, 3, 2 ],
		} );

		await userEvent.click(
			screen.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Remove Budget day live' } )
		);
		expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith( {
			postIds: [ 2, 3 ],
		} );
	} );

	it( 'disables "move up" on the first item', () => {
		render(
			<Edit
				attributes={ { postIds: [ 1, 2 ] } }
				setAttributes={ jest.fn() }
			/>
		);

		expect(
			screen.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Move Budget day live up' } )
		).toBeDisabled();
	} );
} );

How it's tested: 5 passing: debounce timing (proves the cleanup cancels stale timers), search and add, hiding chosen posts, reorder/remove by ID, and a disabled "move up" on the first item.

@wordpress/scripts builds TypeScript out of the box, and most @wordpress/* packages ship their own types.

Example"The value is catching mistakes like saving a string into a number attribute. Without types that only shows up as a broken block in production."
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q21-typescript/

A Reading Time block written in TSX. The attributes are typed once and used by BlockEditProps<T>. type-checks.ts holds deliberate mistakes marked @ts-expect-error. If any of them stopped being an error, tsc would fail, which proves those bugs are caught at compile time.

type-checks.ts: mistakes tsc must reject plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q21-typescript/type-checks.ts
/**
 * Q21: compile-time checks. This file is only read by `npm run typecheck`
 * (tsc --noEmit). Each @ts-expect-error line MUST be an error, or tsc fails:
 * that proves these mistakes are caught before they reach production.
 */
import type { ReadingTimeAttributes } from './types';
import { readingTimeMinutes } from './reading-time';

declare const setAttributes: (
	attrs: Partial< ReadingTimeAttributes >
) => void;

setAttributes( { wordsPerMinute: 250 } ); // OK.

// @ts-expect-error: a string in a number attribute (a broken block in production).
setAttributes( { wordsPerMinute: 'fast' } );

// @ts-expect-error: a typo in the attribute name.
setAttributes( { wordPerMinute: 250 } );

// @ts-expect-error: wrong argument order/types.
readingTimeMinutes( 200, 'some text' );

export {};
edit.tsx: typed props plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q21-typescript/edit.tsx
/**
 * Q21: the Reading Time block's edit component, in TypeScript.
 *
 * The point: the attributes are typed once (types.ts) and every use is checked
 * at compile time (`npm run typecheck` = tsc --noEmit, run in CI). E.g.
 * setAttributes( { wordsPerMinute: 'fast' } ) fails the build here instead of
 * saving a broken block in production. See type-checks.ts.
 *
 * The wp-scripts build compiles .tsx with no extra setup, and the WordPress
 * packages (@wordpress/blocks etc.) ship their own types.
 */

// "import type" is erased at build time: it exists only for the type checker.
import type { BlockEditProps } from '@wordpress/blocks';
import { useBlockProps, InspectorControls } from '@wordpress/block-editor';
import { PanelBody, RangeControl, TextControl } from '@wordpress/components';
import { useSelect } from '@wordpress/data';
import { __, sprintf } from '@wordpress/i18n';
import type { ReadingTimeAttributes } from './types';
import { readingTimeMinutes } from './reading-time';

// BlockEditProps<T> describes what WordPress passes to edit(): attributes of
// type T, and a setAttributes that only accepts a Partial<T>.
type EditProps = BlockEditProps< ReadingTimeAttributes >;
// The 'core/editor' store is selected by name (string), which TypeScript can't
// look up, so describe the one selector we call.
type EditorSelectors = { getEditedPostContent: () => string };

export default function Edit( { attributes, setAttributes }: EditProps ) {
	const { wordsPerMinute, prefix } = attributes;

	// Plain text of the post being edited, for a live preview. The store is
	// selected by name, so type the one selector we use.
	const text = useSelect( ( select ) => {
		const editor = select( 'core/editor' ) as EditorSelectors;
		return editor.getEditedPostContent().replace( /<[^>]+>/g, ' ' );
	}, [] );

	// Same rule as the PHP render (reading_time_minutes in q04), so the editor
	// preview matches the front end.
	const minutes = readingTimeMinutes( text, wordsPerMinute );

	return (
		<>
			<InspectorControls>
				<PanelBody title={ __( 'Settings', 'news-uk' ) }>
					<RangeControl
						__next40pxDefaultSize
						__nextHasNoMarginBottom
						label={ __( 'Words per minute', 'news-uk' ) }
						min={ 100 }
						max={ 400 }
						value={ wordsPerMinute }
						// RangeControl may report undefined (when reset), but the
						// attribute is a number: tsc forces us to handle that case.
						onChange={ ( value ) =>
							setAttributes( { wordsPerMinute: value ?? 200 } )
						}
					/>
					<TextControl
						__next40pxDefaultSize
						__nextHasNoMarginBottom
						label={ __( 'Prefix', 'news-uk' ) }
						value={ prefix }
						onChange={ ( value ) =>
							setAttributes( { prefix: value } )
						}
					/>
				</PanelBody>
			</InspectorControls>
			{ /* useBlockProps: the block's selectable wrapper (see q01 edit.js). */ }
			<p { ...useBlockProps() }>
				{ prefix }
				{ /* sprintf + a translators comment: translators see what %d means. */ }
				{ sprintf(
					/* translators: %d: minutes */
					__( '%d min read', 'news-uk' ),
					minutes
				) }
			</p>
		</>
	);
}

Q22.How do you test a block's edit component with React Testing Library?

"Render the Edit component on its own with test attributes and a mocked setAttributes, act like a user, and assert what changed. I mock the editor-only bits."

Picture thisTesting a car dashboard on a bench. You don't need the whole car: you plug in a fake engine (mocks), press the buttons like a driver would, and check the right lights come on.
jest.mock( '@wordpress/block-editor', () => ( {
  useBlockProps: () => ( {} ),
  InspectorControls: ( { children } ) => <div>{ children }</div>,
} ) );

test( 'toggling "Show image" updates the attribute', async () => {
  const setAttributes = jest.fn();
  render( <Edit attributes={ { showImage: false } } setAttributes={ setAttributes } /> );

  await userEvent.click( screen.getByRole( 'checkbox', { name: 'Show image' } ) );

  expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenCalledWith( { showImage: true } );
} );

test( 'shows a placeholder when no post is selected', () => {
  render( <Edit attributes={ {} } setAttributes={ jest.fn() } /> );
  expect( screen.getByText( /select a post/i ) ).toBeInTheDocument();
} );
  • Isolate: mock useBlockProps and InspectorControls. For useSelect, mock the hook or use a test registry (createRegistry + RegistryProvider).
  • Assert behaviour: setAttributes called correctly, placeholder shown, controls labelled. getByRole with a name also checks accessibility for free.
  • Keep it focused: one behaviour per test, user-event for interactions, no giant snapshots.
Remember it as"Render, Act, Assert, as a user would." If getByRole can't find it, a screen reader can't either.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q22-rtl-edit-test/

The Article Teaser edit component rendered on its own. useBlockProps is mocked, InspectorControls is mocked to render its children in a labelled region, and useSelect returns whatever each test needs. The interactions use user-event, and the queries use getByRole with a name (which also checks labels).

edit.test.js plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q22-rtl-edit-test/edit.test.js
/**
 * Q22: React Testing Library test for the Article Teaser edit component (q01).
 * "Render, Act, Assert, as a user would."
 */

/**
 * External dependencies
 */
import { render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react';
import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';

/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
import { useSelect } from '@wordpress/data';

/**
 * Internal dependencies
 */
import Edit from '../q01-article-teaser/edit';

// Isolate: no block editor around the component. InspectorControls renders
// its children in place (in a labelled region), so sidebar controls are testable.
jest.mock( '@wordpress/block-editor', () => ( {
	useBlockProps: ( props = {} ) => props,
	InspectorControls: ( { children } ) => (
		<div role="region" aria-label="Sidebar">
			{ children }
		</div>
	),
} ) );

// Keep the real store helpers, but control what useSelect returns.
jest.mock( '@wordpress/data', () => ( {
	...jest.requireActual( '@wordpress/data' ),
	useSelect: jest.fn(),
} ) );

describe( 'Article Teaser edit', () => {
	beforeEach( () => {
		useSelect.mockReset();
		useSelect.mockReturnValue( null );
	} );

	it( 'shows a placeholder asking for a post when none is set', () => {
		render( <Edit attributes={ {} } setAttributes={ jest.fn() } /> );

		// One Post ID field in the sidebar, one in the placeholder.
		expect(
			screen.getAllByRole( 'spinbutton', { name: 'Post ID' } )
		).toHaveLength( 2 );
		expect( screen.getByText( 'Article Teaser' ) ).toBeVisible();
	} );

	it( 'stores the typed post ID as a number', async () => {
		const setAttributes = jest.fn();
		render( <Edit attributes={ {} } setAttributes={ setAttributes } /> );

		const sidebar = screen.getByRole( 'region', { name: 'Sidebar' } );
		await userEvent.type(
			within( sidebar ).getByRole( 'spinbutton', { name: 'Post ID' } ),
			'7'
		);

		expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith( { postId: 7 } );
	} );

	it( 'clears the post ID when the field is emptied', async () => {
		const setAttributes = jest.fn();
		render(
			<Edit
				attributes={ { postId: 7 } }
				setAttributes={ setAttributes }
			/>
		);

		await userEvent.clear(
			screen.getByRole( 'spinbutton', { name: 'Post ID' } )
		);

		expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith( {
			postId: undefined,
		} );
	} );

	it( 'toggling "Show image" updates the attribute', async () => {
		const setAttributes = jest.fn();
		render(
			<Edit
				attributes={ { showImage: false } }
				setAttributes={ setAttributes }
			/>
		);

		// getByRole + name also proves the control is labelled for screen readers.
		await userEvent.click(
			screen.getByRole( 'checkbox', { name: 'Show image' } )
		);

		expect( setAttributes ).toHaveBeenCalledWith( { showImage: true } );
	} );

	it( 'shows a loading state while the post is fetched', () => {
		render(
			<Edit attributes={ { postId: 7 } } setAttributes={ jest.fn() } />
		);

		expect( screen.getByText( 'Loading…' ) ).toBeInTheDocument();
	} );

	it( 'shows the selected post title and hides the placeholder', () => {
		useSelect.mockReturnValue( { title: { raw: 'Budget day live' } } );
		render(
			<Edit attributes={ { postId: 7 } } setAttributes={ jest.fn() } />
		);

		expect( screen.getByText( 'Budget day live' ) ).toBeInTheDocument();
		// Only the sidebar field is left: the placeholder is gone.
		expect(
			screen.getAllByRole( 'spinbutton', { name: 'Post ID' } )
		).toHaveLength( 1 );
	} );
} );

How it's tested: 6 passing. Real gotcha found: Placeholder also announces its instructions in an a11y.speak live region, so getByText found the text twice. The test asserts on the fields instead.

Q23.Walk us through a Playwright test for a custom block using @wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright.

"Set up through the API, then do the real editor journey (create post, insert block, interact, publish), then visit the front end and check what a reader sees."

Picture thisA mystery shopper. Back-office staff set up the shop quickly (API setup). Then the shopper walks in and does what a real customer does (UI journey), and finally checks the receipt (front end).
import { test, expect } from '@wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright';

test.describe( 'Article Teaser', () => {
  test.beforeAll( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
    await requestUtils.activatePlugin( 'md-blocks' );
  } );
  test.afterEach( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
    await requestUtils.deleteAllPosts();
  } );

  test( 'can be inserted and published', async ( { admin, editor, page } ) => {
    await admin.createNewPost();
    await editor.insertBlock( { name: 'md/article-teaser' } );
    await editor.canvas.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Select post' } ).click();
    // ...choose a post...
    await expect.poll( editor.getBlocks ).toMatchObject( [ { name: 'md/article-teaser' } ] );

    const postId = await editor.publishPost();
    await page.goto( `/?p=${ postId }` );
    await expect( page.getByRole( 'link', { name: /Hello Times/ } ) ).toBeVisible();
  } );
} );
  • API setup (requestUtils) is fast and keeps every test isolated.
  • editor.canvas: the editor content is inside an iframe, so locate inside it.
  • Config: the default from @wordpress/scripts targets wp-env (WP_BASE_URL), logs in once and saves the session, and keeps traces on failure.
Remember it as"Set up by API, act by UI, check as a reader."
