📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q23-playwright/, playwright.config.js

Set up by API, act by UI, check as a reader. The test inserts the block through the real inserter search, types the post ID inside editor.canvas (the iframe), ticks the sidebar toggle, publishes, then visits the post. A second test proves the title on the front end follows edits without re-saving.

article-teaser.spec.js plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q23-playwright/article-teaser.spec.js
/**
 * Q23: Playwright test for the Article Teaser block (q01).
 * "Set up by API, act by UI, check as a reader."
 */

/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
const { test, expect } = require( '@wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright' );

test.describe( 'Article Teaser', () => {
	let target;

	// Fast, isolated setup through the REST API, not the UI.
	test.beforeEach( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
		target = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			title: 'Budget day live',
			status: 'publish',
		} );
	} );

	test.afterEach( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
		await requestUtils.deleteAllPosts();
	} );

	test( 'editor can insert it from the inserter and publish', async ( {
		admin,
		editor,
		page,
	} ) => {
		await admin.createNewPost( { title: 'Teaser test' } );

		// The real journey: open the inserter and search, like an editor would.
		await page
			.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Block Inserter', exact: true } )
			.click();
		await page
			.getByRole( 'searchbox', { name: 'Search' } )
			.fill( 'Article Teaser' );
		await page
			.getByRole( 'option', { name: 'Article Teaser', exact: true } )
			.click();

		// The editor canvas is an iframe: locate inside editor.canvas.
		const block = editor.canvas.getByRole( 'document', {
			name: 'Block: Article Teaser',
		} );
		await block
			.getByRole( 'spinbutton', { name: 'Post ID' } )
			.fill( String( target.id ) );
		await expect( block.getByText( 'Budget day live' ) ).toBeVisible();

		// Sidebar control.
		await editor.openDocumentSettingsSidebar();
		await page.getByRole( 'checkbox', { name: 'Show image' } ).check();

		await expect.poll( editor.getBlocks ).toMatchObject( [
			{
				name: 'news-uk/article-teaser',
				attributes: { postId: target.id, showImage: true },
			},
		] );

		const postId = await editor.publishPost();

		// Check as a reader.
		await page.goto( `/?p=${ postId }` );
		await expect(
			page.getByRole( 'link', { name: 'Budget day live' } )
		).toHaveAttribute( 'href', target.link );
	} );

	test( 'front end follows title changes without re-saving', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		const host = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			title: 'Host',
			status: 'publish',
			content: `<!-- wp:news-uk/article-teaser {"postId":${ target.id }} /-->`,
		} );

		await requestUtils.rest( {
			method: 'POST',
			path: `/wp/v2/posts/${ target.id }`,
			data: { title: 'Budget day: what it means for you' },
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ host.id }` );
		await expect(
			page.getByRole( 'link', {
				name: 'Budget day: what it means for you',
			} )
		).toBeVisible();
	} );
} );
playwright.config.js: Docker site, specs in question folders, local Chrome playwright.config.js
/**
 * Playwright config: the @wordpress/scripts defaults, but pointed at the
 * per-question folders and at the Docker site instead of wp-env.
 *
 * PW_CHANNEL=chrome runs the installed Google Chrome instead of Playwright's
 * bundled Chromium (useful when the browser download is not available).
 */
const { defineConfig } = require( '@playwright/test' );

// The e2e utils log in with WP_USERNAME/WP_PASSWORD (default admin/password).
// VIP's mu-plugins forbid the username "admin", so the local install uses newsuk.
process.env.WP_USERNAME ??= 'newsuk';
process.env.WP_PASSWORD ??= 'password';
// The Docker site (the @wordpress/scripts default is wp-env's port 8889).
process.env.WP_BASE_URL ??= 'http://localhost:8080';

const baseConfig = require( '@wordpress/scripts/config/playwright.config' );

module.exports = defineConfig( {
	...baseConfig,
	testDir: './plugins/news-uk-practice/src',
	testMatch: '**/*.spec.js',
	webServer: undefined,
	projects: baseConfig.projects.map( ( project ) => ( {
		...project,
		use: { ...project.use, channel: process.env.PW_CHANNEL || undefined },
	} ) ),
} );

Environment gotchas the first run hit:

  • VIP forbids the username admin. wp core install said "success" but created no user. The local admin is now newsuk, and the config sets WP_USERNAME.
  • Core lives in /wp, so WordPress scoped the admin cookie to /wp/wp-admin, but the e2e utils open /wp-admin: the editor looked logged out. Fixed locally with ADMIN_COOKIE_PATH.
  • WordPress 6.9 moved block styles from a "Styles" tab to a panel in the Block tab (fixed in the Q32 spec).

How it's tested: Run: WP_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 PW_CHANNEL=chrome npm run test:e2e. Result: 9 passed in Chrome (Q4 ×2, Q5 ×2, Q23 ×2, Q32 ×3).

✓ ESLint clean✓ Playwright passes (Chrome)

5. Headless and decoupled WordPress

News UK mixes native WordPress on VIP with headless React pages, and is moving features back to native WordPress.

Q24.Be ready to discuss headless WordPress, GraphQL APIs and render services.

"Headless means WordPress only stores and serves content, and a separate app such as React or Next.js draws the pages. You get front-end freedom, but you have to rebuild things WordPress gave you for free."

Picture thisTraditional WordPress is a restaurant that cooks and serves. Headless is a dark kitchen (WordPress) sending food to a separate dining room (the React app). The dining room can look like anything, but now you run two buildings, and the chef can't easily see how the plate looks when it's served (preview).
ProsCons
Any front-end tech
Scale front and back separately		Previews and editor parity are harder
Routing and SEO logic duplicated
More infrastructure to run

GraphQL (vs REST): one endpoint, and the client asks for exactly the fields it needs. That's like ordering à la carte instead of a fixed menu. The catch is caching: every query is different, so you need persisted queries and smart invalidation.

Migrating a headless page back to native WordPress (the News UK direction). Protect four things:

  1. URLs: same paths, or 301 redirects
  2. SEO: same titles, meta, canonical tags, structured data
  3. Analytics: same data layer events so dashboards don't break
  4. Caching: edge cache rules carry over

Then roll out one page type at a time behind a flag, and compare before and after.

Remember it as"Dark kitchen." Migration checklist: "URLs, SEO, Analytics, Cache."
Your storyZiff Davis: a GraphQL catalogue microservice behind a Node.js caching layer, consumed by a WordPress VIP multisite.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q24-headless/

Both sides of headless. render-service.mjs is a tiny Node "dining room" that draws pages from the REST API; note everything it has to rebuild by hand. In WordPress, one REST field carries everything the app needs, Preview goes to the app, and for the move back to native there are 301s from the app's /article/{slug} URLs.

render-service.mjs: run with node plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q24-headless/render-service.mjs
/**
 * Q24: a tiny headless "render service". WordPress only stores and serves
 * content (REST API); this separate Node app draws the pages.
 *
 *   node plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q24-headless/render-service.mjs
 *   open http://localhost:3001/article/<post-slug>
 *
 * Note everything it has to rebuild that WordPress gave us for free: routing,
 * escaping, the template, 404s, caching headers, SEO tags.
 */
import { createServer } from 'node:http';

const WP = process.env.WP_URL || 'http://localhost:8080';
const PORT = Number( process.env.PORT || 3001 );

const escape = ( value ) =>
	String( value ).replace(
		/[&<>"']/g,
		( c ) =>
			( {
				'&': '&amp;',
				'<': '&lt;',
				'>': '&gt;',
				'"': '&quot;',
				"'": '&#39;',
			} )[ c ]
	);

async function getArticle( slug ) {
	// Ask only for the fields the page needs (REST's answer to GraphQL field selection).
	const url = new URL( '/wp-json/wp/v2/posts', WP );
	url.searchParams.set( 'slug', slug );
	url.searchParams.set( '_fields', 'title,content,link,news_uk' );

	const response = await fetch( url, {
		signal: AbortSignal.timeout( 3000 ),
	} );
	if ( ! response.ok ) {
		throw new Error( `WordPress returned ${ response.status }` );
	}
	const [ post ] = await response.json();
	return post;
}

function page( post ) {
	return `<!doctype html>
<html lang="en"><head><meta charset="utf-8">
<title>${ escape( post.title.rendered ) }</title>
<link rel="canonical" href="${ escape( post.news_uk.canonical ) }">
</head><body>
<h1>${ post.title.rendered }</h1>
<p><em>${ escape( post.news_uk.standfirst ) }</em> · ${ post.news_uk.reading_time } min read</p>
${ post.content.rendered }
<footer>Rendered by the headless render service</footer>
</body></html>`;
}

createServer( async ( req, res ) => {
	const match = req.url.match( /^\/article\/([a-z0-9-]+)\/?$/ );
	if ( ! match ) {
		res.writeHead( 404 ).end( 'Not found' );
		return;
	}

	try {
		const post = await getArticle( match[ 1 ] );
		if ( ! post ) {
			res.writeHead( 404 ).end( 'Not found' );
			return;
		}
		res.writeHead( 200, {
			'Content-Type': 'text/html; charset=utf-8',
			// Edge caching has to be set up by hand, too.
			'Cache-Control': 'public, max-age=60, stale-while-revalidate=300',
		} ).end( page( post ) );
	} catch {
		// API down or slow: fail fast rather than hang the reader.
		res.writeHead( 502 ).end( 'Upstream error' );
	}
} ).listen( PORT, () => {
	// eslint-disable-next-line no-console
	console.log(
		`Render service on http://localhost:${ PORT } (WordPress: ${ WP })`
	);
} );
Migration: 301 from old app URLs plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q24-headless/load.php
function legacy_redirect_target( $path ) {
	if ( ! preg_match( '#^/article/([a-z0-9-]+)/?$#', $path, $match ) ) {
		return null;
	}

	// Core's get_page_by_path() is cached since WordPress 6.1, so VIP deprecated
	// its wpcom_vip_get_page_by_path() wrapper. Use core.
	$post = get_page_by_path( $match[1], OBJECT, 'post' );

	return ( $post && 'publish' === $post->post_status ) ? get_permalink( $post ) : null;
}

/**
 * Issue the 301 before WordPress decides it's a 404.
 *
 * @return void
 */
function redirect_legacy_urls() {
	if ( ! is_404() ) {
		return;
	}

	$path   = wp_parse_url( esc_url_raw( wp_unslash( $_SERVER['REQUEST_URI'] ?? '' ) ), PHP_URL_PATH ); // phpcs:ignore WordPressVIPMinimum.Variables.ServerVariables.UserControlledHeaders
	$target = legacy_redirect_target( (string) $path );

	if ( $target ) {
		wp_safe_redirect( $target, 301, 'News UK headless migration' );
		exit;
	}
}
add_action( 'template_redirect', __NAMESPACE__ . '\redirect_legacy_urls' );
PHPUnit plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q24-headless/tests/class-headless-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q24: REST field for headless clients, preview link, and migration redirects.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q24\legacy_redirect_target;

/**
 * Headless tests.
 */
class Headless_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Pretty permalinks, like the real site.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		$this->set_permalink_structure( '/%postname%/' );
	}

	/**
	 * The REST API carries what the app needs in one request.
	 */
	public function test_rest_field_for_headless_app() {
		$id = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_content' => str_repeat( 'word ', 300 ) ] );
		update_post_meta( $id, 'news_uk_standfirst', 'Short summary' );

		$data = rest_do_request( '/wp/v2/posts/' . $id )->get_data();

		$this->assertSame( 'Short summary', $data['news_uk']['standfirst'] );
		$this->assertSame( 2, $data['news_uk']['reading_time'] );
		$this->assertSame( get_permalink( $id ), $data['news_uk']['canonical'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Old app URLs map to native permalinks; anything else is left alone.
	 */
	public function test_legacy_urls_map_to_native_permalinks() {
		$id = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_name' => 'rates-decision' ] );

		$this->assertSame( get_permalink( $id ), legacy_redirect_target( '/article/rates-decision' ) );
		$this->assertSame( get_permalink( $id ), legacy_redirect_target( '/article/rates-decision/' ) );
		$this->assertNull( legacy_redirect_target( '/article/does-not-exist' ) );
		$this->assertNull( legacy_redirect_target( '/rates-decision/' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Drafts are not redirected to (no leaking unpublished URLs).
	 */
	public function test_drafts_are_not_redirected_to() {
		self::factory()->post->create(
			[
				'post_name'   => 'secret-draft',
				'post_status' => 'draft',
			]
		);

		$this->assertNull( legacy_redirect_target( '/article/secret-draft' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Preview goes to the app only when one is configured.
	 */
	public function test_preview_link_points_to_app_when_configured() {
		$id = self::factory()->post->create();

		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( 'app.example.com', get_preview_post_link( $id ) );

		add_filter( 'news_uk_headless_url', fn() => 'https://app.example.com' );
		$this->assertStringStartsWith( 'https://app.example.com/preview?id=' . $id, get_preview_post_link( $id ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: PHPUnit covers the REST field, URL mapping (drafts are never a redirect target) and the preview link. The redirect uses core get_page_by_path(): the VIP mu-plugins reported wpcom_vip_get_page_by_path() as deprecated during the test run.

✓ ESLint clean✓ PHPUnit passes

Q25.How would you integrate an external GraphQL API into WordPress so that pages stay fast and stay up when the API is slow or down?

"Never call the API while a reader waits. Sync a local copy in the background, render from that copy, and if the API goes down readers just see the last good data."

Picture thisA shop with a stockroom. You don't phone the supplier every time a customer asks for something. A delivery van (cron or webhook) restocks the stockroom regularly. If the supplier's phone line is down, customers can still buy what's on the shelf.
  1. Fetch in the background: cron or a webhook, server side, with a timeout.
  2. Store a local copy of what the front end needs (custom table or post meta).
  3. Sync by delta: only fetch what changed, and invalidate the cache per item.
  4. Render from the local copy, cached in the object cache. An outage means slightly stale data, not an error page.
  5. Monitor: log failures, alert through New Relic.
  6. Test: PHPUnit, mocking the API with the pre_http_request filter (success, timeout, 500, bad JSON).
add_filter( 'pre_http_request', fn() => new WP_Error( 'http_request_failed', 'timeout' ) );
md_sync_products();
$this->assertSame( 'Old price £99', md_render_product( 42 ) ); // still serves last good data
Remember it as"Stockroom, not phone call."
Your storyThis is your Ziff Davis design: 28k products, delta-based sync, per-product invalidation, cache-aware rendering. Tell it as a story with those numbers.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q25-external-graphql/

Stockroom, not phone call. Cron runs a delta sync (it sends the last updatedAt cursor) into a private post type, invalidates only the changed product's cache key, and moves the cursor only after everything is saved. Readers read the local copy. Every failure mode returns a WP_Error and leaves the data untouched. A local-only fake GraphQL endpoint lets you demo it, including an outage.

sync.php: delta sync with a timeout plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q25-external-graphql/sync.php
function sync_products() {
	$since    = get_option( CURSOR_KEY, '' );
	$response = wp_remote_post(
		endpoint(),
		[
			'timeout' => 3, // Short: a slow API must not tie up PHP workers.
			'headers' => [ 'Content-Type' => 'application/json' ],
			'body'    => wp_json_encode(
				[
					'query'     => QUERY,
					'variables' => [ 'since' => $since ? $since : null ],
				]
			),
		]
	);

	$products = parse_response( $response );

	if ( is_wp_error( $products ) ) {
		/**
		 * Hook for logging/alerting (New Relic, Slack...). Readers never see it.
		 */
		do_action( 'news_uk_catalogue_sync_failed', $products );
		return $products;
	}

	$cursor = $since;
	foreach ( $products as $item ) {
		save_product( $item );
		$cursor = max( $cursor, (string) $item['updatedAt'] );
	}

	// Only move the cursor after everything saved, so a crash re-fetches.
	update_option( CURSOR_KEY, $cursor, false );

	return count( $products );
}

/**
 * Turn an HTTP response into a list of products, or a WP_Error for every way it can fail.
 *
 * @param array|\WP_Error $response wp_remote_post() result.
 * @return array|\WP_Error
 */
function parse_response( $response ) {
	if ( is_wp_error( $response ) ) {
		return $response; // Timeout, DNS, connection refused.
	}

	$code = wp_remote_retrieve_response_code( $response );
	if ( 200 !== $code ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_catalogue_http', 'Catalogue API returned HTTP ' . $code );
	}

	$body = json_decode( wp_remote_retrieve_body( $response ), true );
	if ( ! is_array( $body ) ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_catalogue_json', 'Catalogue API returned invalid JSON' );
	}

	// GraphQL reports errors with HTTP 200 and an "errors" array.
	if ( ! empty( $body['errors'] ) ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_catalogue_graphql', $body['errors'][0]['message'] ?? 'GraphQL error' );
	}

	if ( ! isset( $body['data']['products'] ) || ! is_array( $body['data']['products'] ) ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_catalogue_shape', 'Unexpected response shape' );
	}

	return $body['data']['products'];
}
catalogue.php: cached read, per-product invalidation plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q25-external-graphql/catalogue.php
function get_product( $external_id ) {
	$external_id = sanitize_key( $external_id );
	$cached      = wp_cache_get( 'product_' . $external_id, CACHE_GROUP );

	if ( false !== $cached ) {
		return $cached ? $cached : null; // Empty array = cached "not found".
	}

	$ids = get_posts(
		[
			'post_type'        => POST_TYPE,
			'name'             => $external_id,
			'post_status'      => 'publish',
			'posts_per_page'   => 1,
			'fields'           => 'ids',
			'no_found_rows'    => true,
			'suppress_filters' => false,
		]
	);

	$product = $ids ? [
		'id'       => $external_id,
		'name'     => get_the_title( $ids[0] ),
		'price'    => (float) get_post_meta( $ids[0], 'price', true ),
		'currency' => get_post_meta( $ids[0], 'currency', true ),
		'url'      => get_post_meta( $ids[0], 'url', true ),
	] : [];

	wp_cache_set( 'product_' . $external_id, $product, CACHE_GROUP, HOUR_IN_SECONDS );

	return $product ? $product : null;
}

/**
 * Insert or update one product from API data, and invalidate only its cache key.
 *
 * @param array $item One product from the API.
 * @return int|\WP_Error Post ID.
 */
function save_product( array $item ) {
	$slug     = sanitize_key( $item['id'] );
	$existing = get_posts(
		[
			'post_type'        => POST_TYPE,
			'name'             => $slug,
			'post_status'      => 'any',
			'posts_per_page'   => 1,
			'fields'           => 'ids',
			'no_found_rows'    => true,
			'suppress_filters' => false,
		]
	);

	$post_id = wp_insert_post(
		[
			'ID'          => $existing ? $existing[0] : 0,
			'post_type'   => POST_TYPE,
			'post_status' => 'publish',
			'post_name'   => $slug,
			'post_title'  => sanitize_text_field( $item['name'] ),
			'meta_input'  => [
				'price'    => (float) $item['price'],
				'currency' => sanitize_text_field( $item['currency'] ),
				'url'      => esc_url_raw( $item['url'] ),
			],
		],
		true
	);

	// Per-product invalidation: the other 27,999 products stay warm.
	wp_cache_delete( 'product_' . $slug, CACHE_GROUP );

	return $post_id;
}
PHPUnit: pre_http_request for every failure mode plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q25-external-graphql/tests/class-catalogue-sync-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q25: the sync, tested by faking the HTTP layer with pre_http_request.
 * Success, delta, timeout, HTTP 500, bad JSON and GraphQL errors.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q25\get_product;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q25\sync_products;

/**
 * Catalogue sync tests.
 */
class Catalogue_Sync_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Request bodies the "API" received.
	 *
	 * @var array
	 */
	private $requests = [];

	/**
	 * Make the next HTTP call return $result.
	 *
	 * @param array|\WP_Error $result Fake response.
	 * @return void
	 */
	private function fake_api( $result ) {
		remove_all_filters( 'pre_http_request' );
		add_filter(
			'pre_http_request',
			function ( $pre, $args ) use ( $result ) {
				$this->requests[] = json_decode( $args['body'], true );
				return $result;
			},
			10,
			2
		);
	}

	/**
	 * A 200 response with the given products.
	 *
	 * @param array $products Products.
	 * @return array
	 */
	private function ok( array $products ) {
		return [
			'response' => [ 'code' => 200 ],
			'body'     => wp_json_encode( [ 'data' => [ 'products' => $products ] ] ),
		];
	}

	/**
	 * One product as the API sends it.
	 *
	 * @param float  $price      Price.
	 * @param string $updated_at Change time.
	 * @return array
	 */
	private function iphone( $price, $updated_at ) {
		return [
			'id'        => 'iphone-16',
			'name'      => 'iPhone 16',
			'price'     => $price,
			'currency'  => 'GBP',
			'url'       => 'https://shop.example.com/iphone-16',
			'updatedAt' => $updated_at,
		];
	}

	/**
	 * First sync stores products locally.
	 */
	public function test_sync_stores_products() {
		$this->fake_api( $this->ok( [ $this->iphone( 799, '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z' ) ] ) );

		$this->assertSame( 1, sync_products() );
		$this->assertSame( 799.0, get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Second sync asks only for changes and invalidates just that product.
	 */
	public function test_delta_sync_sends_cursor_and_refreshes_cache() {
		$this->fake_api( $this->ok( [ $this->iphone( 799, '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z' ) ] ) );
		sync_products();
		get_product( 'iphone-16' ); // Warm the cache.

		$this->fake_api( $this->ok( [ $this->iphone( 749, '2026-02-01T00:00:00Z' ) ] ) );
		sync_products();

		$this->assertSame( '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z', end( $this->requests )['variables']['since'] );
		$this->assertSame( 749.0, get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'], 'Cache was invalidated.' );
	}

	/**
	 * Every failure mode leaves the last good data in place.
	 *
	 * @dataProvider failures
	 *
	 * @param array|\WP_Error $response   Fake response.
	 * @param string          $error_code Expected WP_Error code.
	 */
	public function test_failures_keep_last_good_data( $response, $error_code ) {
		$this->fake_api( $this->ok( [ $this->iphone( 799, '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z' ) ] ) );
		sync_products();

		$this->fake_api( $response );
		$result = sync_products();

		$this->assertWPError( $result );
		$this->assertSame( $error_code, $result->get_error_code() );
		$this->assertSame( 799.0, get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'] );
		$this->assertSame( '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z', get_option( 'news_uk_catalogue_cursor' ), 'Cursor did not move.' );
	}

	/**
	 * Failure modes.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function failures() {
		return [
			'timeout'        => [ new \WP_Error( 'http_request_failed', 'cURL error 28: Operation timed out' ), 'http_request_failed' ],
			'HTTP 500'       => [
				[
					'response' => [ 'code' => 500 ],
					'body'     => 'oops',
				],
				'news_uk_catalogue_http',
			],
			'bad JSON'       => [
				[
					'response' => [ 'code' => 200 ],
					'body'     => '<html>',
				],
				'news_uk_catalogue_json',
			],
			'GraphQL errors' => [
				[
					'response' => [ 'code' => 200 ],
					'body'     => '{"errors":[{"message":"Rate limited"}]}',
				],
				'news_uk_catalogue_graphql',
			],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Failures fire the alerting hook.
	 */
	public function test_failure_fires_alert_hook() {
		$this->fake_api( new \WP_Error( 'http_request_failed', 'timeout' ) );
		sync_products();

		$this->assertSame( 1, did_action( 'news_uk_catalogue_sync_failed' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Cached reads do not touch the database.
	 */
	public function test_cached_read_skips_database() {
		global $wpdb;
		$this->fake_api( $this->ok( [ $this->iphone( 799, '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z' ) ] ) );
		sync_products();
		get_product( 'iphone-16' );

		$before = $wpdb->num_queries;
		get_product( 'iphone-16' );

		$this->assertSame( $before, $wpdb->num_queries );
	}
}

6. Architecture scenarios

Principal engineers like "what would you do if" questions. Think out loud: constraints, two options, trade-off, choice, how you'd test it.

Q26.How do you make and document architecture decisions?

"Requirements and constraints first, at least two options with trade-offs, then the decision written as a short ADR in the repo, and a small spike to prove it before the whole team commits."

Picture thisBuying a house. Budget and must-haves first (constraints). View at least two (options). Write down why you chose this one (ADR), so in five years nobody asks "why on earth did we buy next to a railway?" Spend a night there first (spike).
  • Constraints: traffic, editorial workflow, caching, VIP rules, accessibility, and lifespan: enterprise sites live 5 to 10 years.
  • Compare on: maintenance cost, performance, editor experience, testability, migration risk. Prefer WordPress-native APIs and fewer plugins.
  • ADR: Context, Options, Decision, Consequences. Short, in the repo, linked from the PR.
  • Spike behind a feature flag before committing everyone.
Example ADR (say it in 20 seconds)Context: we need custom fields for article standfirsts. Options: ACF, or registered post meta with Block Bindings. Decision: post meta + bindings. Consequences: no plugin dependency, data in REST, core blocks reused; editors get a slightly less fancy UI for complex fields.
Remember it as"Constrain, Compare, Capture, Check."

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q26-architecture-decisions/

A real ADR (Context, Options, Decision, Consequences) for the Q4 choice, kept in the repo, plus the feature-flag helper used to spike it.

adr/0001-standfirst-post-meta-and-block-bindings.md plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q26-architecture-decisions/adr/0001-standfirst-post-meta-and-block-bindings.md
# ADR 0001: Standfirst as registered post meta with Block Bindings

- Status: Accepted
- Date: 2026-11-12
- Deciders: principal engineer, WordPress team

## Context

Articles need a standfirst (the summary under the headline). Editors must be
able to type it on the page, the headless app needs it in the REST API, and
the field will live for 5 to 10 years. We want as few plugins as possible on VIP.

## Options

1. **ACF field group + ACF block** (`src/q04-block-bindings/acf.php`)
   - Familiar UI, fast to build.
   - Adds a plugin dependency and licence; field definitions live in ACF.
2. **Registered post meta + Block Bindings** (`src/q04-block-bindings/native.php`)
   - Core only. Data typed, sanitised and in REST. Core Paragraph reused.
   - Less fancy UI for complex fields (repeaters, galleries).
3. **Custom block with its own attribute**
   - Full control, but the value is locked inside post_content, not queryable.

## Decision

Option 2. Behind the `native-standfirst` feature flag for the first sprint so
we can compare with the ACF version on a few templates (the spike).

## Consequences

- No new plugin; one less thing to update and security-review.
- Standfirst is in REST for the headless app (`news_uk.standfirst`, q24).
- Complex repeating fields would still need a custom UI: revisit if that comes up.
- Tests: `q04-block-bindings/tests` and `q04-block-bindings/e2e`.
feature_enabled(): constant, then option, then filter plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q26-architecture-decisions/load.php
function feature_enabled( $flag ) {
	$constant = 'NEWS_UK_FEATURE_' . strtoupper( str_replace( '-', '_', $flag ) );

	if ( defined( $constant ) ) {
		$enabled = (bool) constant( $constant );
	} else {
		$enabled = in_array( $flag, (array) get_option( 'news_uk_features', [] ), true );
	}

	/**
	 * Last word, e.g. for tests or a percentage rollout.
	 */
	return (bool) apply_filters( 'news_uk_feature_enabled', $enabled, $flag );
}

How it's tested: The test checks the flag sources and that the ADR has its four sections.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q27.Breaking news: traffic jumps tenfold in five minutes on one story, and the live blog block is slowing the site. What do you do?

"Find the real bottleneck with data, protect the page with caching, take expensive work out of the request, and afterwards write it up and add tests so the next big story is fine."

Picture thisA burst pipe. 1) Find where it's leaking (data). 2) Put a bucket under it now (cache). 3) Fix the pipe (move work out of the request). 4) Afterwards, work out why it burst and add a pressure valve (post-mortem, load test).
  1. Diagnose: New Relic, Query Monitor, VIP dashboards. Is it uncached queries, remote calls, or misses at the edge?
  2. Protect: make sure the article is edge cached with a short TTL (say 30 to 60 seconds: fresh enough for a live blog, and absorbs most traffic). Cache the live blog fragment in the object cache and invalidate it on update.
  3. Stampede guard: when a hot key expires, thousands of requests rebuild it at once. Use a lock, or serve stale while one request regenerates.
  4. Remove writes on GET: view counters and similar shouldn't write to the database per page view.
  5. Afterwards: post-incident report, a regression test, a load test.
Remember it as"Find, Shield, Fix, Learn."
Your storyWoo Marketplace incidents you led end to end, including stakeholder updates and post-incident reports.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q27-traffic-spike/

Shield: live blogs get max-age=60, stale-while-revalidate, stale-if-error, and fragments go through remember(). That uses a wp_cache_add lock so one request rebuilds while everyone else gets the stale copy. Fix: views go to a Memcached counter through a REST beacon, and cron flushes them to meta, so GET requests never write to the DB.

remember(): stampede guard plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q27-traffic-spike/load.php
function remember( $key, $ttl, callable $generate ) {
	$entry = wp_cache_get( $key, GROUP );

	if ( is_array( $entry ) && $entry['fresh_until'] > time() ) {
		return $entry['value']; // Fresh: the common case.
	}

	// wp_cache_add only succeeds if the key does not exist: an atomic lock.
	$got_lock = wp_cache_add( $key . ':lock', 1, GROUP, 30 );

	if ( ! $got_lock && is_array( $entry ) ) {
		return $entry['value']; // Someone else is rebuilding: serve stale.
	}

	$value = $generate();
	wp_cache_set(
		$key,
		[
			'value'       => $value,
			'fresh_until' => time() + $ttl,
		],
		GROUP,
		// Keep stale copies around long enough to serve during rebuilds. The sniff
		// can't evaluate a variable; with the 60s live-blog TTL this is 600s.
		$ttl * 10 // phpcs:ignore WordPressVIPMinimum.Performance.LowExpiryCacheTime.CacheTimeUndetermined
	);
	wp_cache_delete( $key . ':lock', GROUP );

	return $value;
}
No writes on GET: record_view() + flush_views() plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q27-traffic-spike/load.php
function record_view( $post_id ) {
	$key = 'views_' . (int) $post_id;

	if ( false === wp_cache_add( $key, 1, GROUP, DAY_IN_SECONDS ) ) {
		return (int) wp_cache_incr( $key, 1, GROUP );
	}

	$pending   = (array) wp_cache_get( 'pending_ids', GROUP );
	$pending[] = (int) $post_id;
	wp_cache_set( 'pending_ids', array_unique( $pending ), GROUP, DAY_IN_SECONDS );

	return 1;
}

/**
 * Cron: flush pending view counts to post meta in one batch.
 *
 * @return void
 */
function flush_views() {
	$pending = (array) wp_cache_get( 'pending_ids', GROUP );
	wp_cache_delete( 'pending_ids', GROUP );

	foreach ( $pending as $post_id ) {
		$count = (int) wp_cache_get( 'views_' . $post_id, GROUP );
		wp_cache_delete( 'views_' . $post_id, GROUP );

		if ( $count > 0 ) {
			update_post_meta( $post_id, 'news_uk_views', (int) get_post_meta( $post_id, 'news_uk_views', true ) + $count );
		}
	}
}
add_action( 'news_uk_flush_views', __NAMESPACE__ . '\flush_views' );
PHPUnit plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q27-traffic-spike/tests/class-traffic-spike-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q27: stampede guard, live blog cache headers, and no writes on GET.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q27\cache_headers;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q27\flush_views;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q27\record_view;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q27\remember;

/**
 * Traffic spike tests.
 */
class Traffic_Spike_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Fresh values are served without rebuilding.
	 */
	public function test_fresh_value_is_reused() {
		$builds = 0;
		$build  = function () use ( &$builds ) {
			return ++$builds;
		};

		remember( 'fragment_a', 60, $build );
		remember( 'fragment_a', 60, $build );

		$this->assertSame( 1, $builds );
	}

	/**
	 * Stale + someone else holds the lock: serve stale, don't rebuild.
	 */
	public function test_stale_value_served_while_another_request_rebuilds() {
		wp_cache_set(
			'fragment_b',
			[
				'value'       => 'old',
				'fresh_until' => time() - 1,
			],
			'news_uk_live'
		);
		wp_cache_add( 'fragment_b:lock', 1, 'news_uk_live' ); // Another request is rebuilding.

		$value = remember( 'fragment_b', 60, fn() => $this->fail( 'Must not rebuild while locked.' ) );

		$this->assertSame( 'old', $value );
	}

	/**
	 * Stale and no lock: this request rebuilds and releases the lock.
	 */
	public function test_stale_value_rebuilt_by_one_request() {
		wp_cache_set(
			'fragment_c',
			[
				'value'       => 'old',
				'fresh_until' => time() - 1,
			],
			'news_uk_live'
		);

		$this->assertSame( 'new', remember( 'fragment_c', 60, fn() => 'new' ) );
		$this->assertFalse( wp_cache_get( 'fragment_c:lock', 'news_uk_live' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Live blogs get a short public cache; other posts are untouched.
	 */
	public function test_live_blog_cache_headers() {
		$live   = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'tags_input' => [ 'live' ] ] );
		$normal = self::factory()->post->create();

		$this->go_to( get_permalink( $live ) );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'max-age=60', cache_headers( [] )['Cache-Control'] );

		$this->go_to( get_permalink( $normal ) );
		$this->assertArrayNotHasKey( 'Cache-Control', cache_headers( [] ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Views: no DB write per view; one write per post per flush.
	 */
	public function test_views_are_batched_not_written_per_request() {
		global $wpdb;
		$id = self::factory()->post->create();

		$before = $wpdb->num_queries;
		foreach ( range( 1, 50 ) as $i ) {
			record_view( $id );
		}
		$this->assertSame( $before, $wpdb->num_queries, '50 views, zero DB queries.' );

		flush_views();
		$this->assertSame( '50', get_post_meta( $id, 'news_uk_views', true ) );
	}
}

How it's tested: The key test: 50 views make zero DB queries, then one flush writes 50. These tests only passed once PHPUnit really used Memcached (see Q10): wp_cache_add() locks and wp_cache_incr() counters need a working cache server. A good reason to test against the real stack. Trade-off to mention: every post meta write bumps the posts last_changed key, which invalidates every cached WP_Query on the site. That's why views are flushed in one batch by cron, not written per view.

✓ PHPUnit passes

Q28.You must share core content or data across several sites in a multisite network, while each brand can override parts of it. How would you design it?

"One canonical record at network level, plus small per-site overrides that win when they exist. Cache per site, invalidate both layers, and test every merge case."

Picture thisA franchise menu. Head office sets the burger recipe and base price (shared). Each branch can change the price or the name on its own menu board (override). If the branch hasn't changed it, head office's version shows. It works just like CSS: a specific rule beats the global one.
  • Separate the layers: network-level canonical store + site-level overrides that take precedence.
  • Decide the source of truth and the sync direction.
  • Cache per site, and invalidate both layers when either changes.
  • Editor UX: blocks and sidebar panels, not raw meta boxes. Shared blocks are identical on every site.
  • Test merge rules with a PHPUnit data provider: shared only, override only, both, missing.
Example"iPhone 16 lives once in the network catalogue: specs, base price, image. CNET overrides the display name and the affiliate link. PCMag overrides nothing, so it shows the shared record. Price changes once and every site picks it up, but CNET's custom link stays."
Remember it as"Head office menu, local chalkboard."
Your storyZiff Davis network-wide product table with brand-level overrides for monetisation and display. This is the iPhone example you've used before.
Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q28-multisite-overrides/

Head office menu, local chalkboard. A pure merge(): overrides win field by field, empty means "use shared", unknown fields are ignored, and an override with no shared record shows nothing. The cache keys include both layers' version numbers, so changing either layer invalidates them automatically.

merge() + get_product() plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q28-multisite-overrides/load.php
function merge( $shared, $override ) {
	if ( null === $shared ) {
		return null; // Overrides alone are orphans: nothing to show.
	}

	$override = array_filter( (array) $override, fn( $value ) => '' !== $value && null !== $value );

	return array_merge( $shared, array_intersect_key( $override, $shared ) );
}

/**
 * Product as this site should show it.
 *
 * @param string $id Product ID.
 * @return array|null
 */
function get_product( $id ) {
	$key    = 'product_' . $id . '_' . version( 'shared' ) . '_' . version( 'site' );
	$cached = wp_cache_get( $key, 'news_uk_network' . get_current_blog_id() );

	if ( false !== $cached ) {
		return $cached;
	}

	$shared   = get_site_option( SHARED_KEY, [] )[ $id ] ?? null;
	$override = get_option( OVERRIDE_KEY, [] )[ $id ] ?? null;
	$product  = merge( $shared, $override );

	wp_cache_set( $key, $product, 'news_uk_network' . get_current_blog_id(), HOUR_IN_SECONDS );

	return $product;
}
PHPUnit: the iPhone example as a data provider plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q28-multisite-overrides/tests/class-overrides-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q28: every merge case with a data provider, then the cache invalidation.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_UnitTestCase;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q28\get_product;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q28\merge;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q28\update_override;
use function News_UK_Practice\Q28\update_shared;

/**
 * Overrides tests.
 */
class Overrides_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	const IPHONE = [
		'name'  => 'iPhone 16',
		'price' => '799',
		'link'  => 'https://shop.example.com/iphone-16',
	];

	/**
	 * Merge rules.
	 *
	 * @dataProvider merge_cases
	 *
	 * @param array|null $shared   Shared.
	 * @param array|null $override Override.
	 * @param array|null $expected Expected.
	 */
	public function test_merge( $shared, $override, $expected ) {
		$this->assertSame( $expected, merge( $shared, $override ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Shared only, override only, both, missing, and the tricky ones.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function merge_cases() {
		return [
			'shared only (PCMag)'     => [ self::IPHONE, null, self::IPHONE ],
			'override wins (CNET)'    => [
				self::IPHONE,
				[
					'name' => 'Apple iPhone 16',
					'link' => 'https://cnet.example/aff/iphone',
				],
				[
					'name'  => 'Apple iPhone 16',
					'price' => '799',
					'link'  => 'https://cnet.example/aff/iphone',
				],
			],
			'empty override = shared' => [ self::IPHONE, [ 'name' => '' ], self::IPHONE ],
			'unknown fields ignored'  => [ self::IPHONE, [ 'colour' => 'red' ], self::IPHONE ],
			'override only = nothing' => [ null, [ 'name' => 'Orphan' ], null ],
			'neither = nothing'       => [ null, null, null ],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Changing either layer shows up immediately, even with a warm cache.
	 */
	public function test_both_layers_invalidate_the_cache() {
		update_shared( 'iphone-16', self::IPHONE );
		$this->assertSame( '799', get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'] ); // Cache now warm.

		update_shared( 'iphone-16', array_merge( self::IPHONE, [ 'price' => '749' ] ) );
		$this->assertSame( '749', get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'], 'Head office price change.' );

		update_override( 'iphone-16', [ 'name' => 'Apple iPhone 16' ] );
		$this->assertSame( 'Apple iPhone 16', get_product( 'iphone-16' )['name'], 'Brand override.' );
		$this->assertSame( '749', get_product( 'iphone-16' )['price'], 'Price still shared.' );
	}
}

How it's tested: Honest limit: the local site isn't multisite, so get_site_option falls back to normal options. The code is the same, but run it on a multisite dev-env before claiming it was tested across sites.

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q31-incident-cli/

"Measure before you touch" as a WP-CLI command, using a migration incident as the example: standfirsts were lost, and the old app had kept them in the excerpt. audit lists the affected posts (or exports CSV), repair is a dry run by default, and --apply writes and leaves an audit trail. The command uses keyset batches (never -1) and is safe to run twice. The logic sits in a plain class so PHPUnit can test it.

class-command.php: wp news-uk standfirst audit|repair [--apply] plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q31-incident-cli/class-command.php
	public function repair( $args, $assoc_args ) {
		$apply = \WP_CLI\Utils\get_flag_value( $assoc_args, 'apply', false );

		if ( ! $apply ) {
			\WP_CLI::warning( 'Dry run: nothing will be written. Re-run with --apply.' );
		}

		$count = ( new Standfirst_Repair() )->repair(
			$apply,
			fn( $id, $value ) => \WP_CLI::log( sprintf( '%s #%d: "%s"', $apply ? 'Repaired' : 'Would repair', $id, $value ) )
		);

		\WP_CLI::success( sprintf( '%d posts %s.', $count, $apply ? 'repaired' : 'would be repaired' ) );
class-standfirst-repair.php: batched, auditable repair plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q31-incident-cli/class-standfirst-repair.php
<?php
/**
 * Q31: the repair logic, kept free of WP-CLI so it can be unit tested.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Q31;

/**
 * Finds and repairs posts whose standfirst was lost in the migration
 * (the old app kept it in the excerpt).
 */
class Standfirst_Repair {

	/**
	 * Posts per batch: bounded memory, never posts_per_page => -1.
	 */
	const BATCH = 100;

	/**
	 * One batch of affected posts, after a given ID (keyset pagination: stable
	 * even while we write, unlike OFFSET).
	 *
	 * @param int $after_id Last ID of the previous batch.
	 * @return \WP_Post[]
	 */
	public function find_batch( $after_id = 0 ) {
		global $wpdb;

		// phpcs:ignore WordPress.DB.DirectDatabaseQuery -- One-off repair, indexed columns, batched.
		$ids = $wpdb->get_col(
			$wpdb->prepare(
				"SELECT p.ID FROM {$wpdb->posts} p
				LEFT JOIN {$wpdb->postmeta} m ON m.post_id = p.ID AND m.meta_key = 'news_uk_standfirst'
				WHERE p.post_type = 'post' AND p.post_status IN ('publish','future','draft')
				AND p.post_excerpt <> '' AND ( m.meta_value IS NULL OR m.meta_value = '' )
				AND p.ID > %d ORDER BY p.ID ASC LIMIT %d",
				$after_id,
				self::BATCH
			)
		);

		return array_map( 'get_post', array_map( 'intval', $ids ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Audit: every affected post, as rows for a report. Changes nothing.
	 *
	 * @return array[] Rows: ID, title, proposed standfirst.
	 */
	public function audit() {
		$rows  = [];
		$after = 0;

		do {
			$batch = $this->find_batch( $after );
			foreach ( $batch as $post ) {
				$rows[] = [
					'ID'         => $post->ID,
					'title'      => $post->post_title,
					'standfirst' => $this->proposed_standfirst( $post ),
				];
				$after  = $post->ID;
			}
			// A full batch means there may be more; a short one means we're done.
			$has_more = count( $batch ) === self::BATCH;
		} while ( $has_more );

		return $rows;
	}

	/**
	 * What we would write.
	 *
	 * @param \WP_Post $post Post.
	 * @return string
	 */
	public function proposed_standfirst( \WP_Post $post ) {
		return sanitize_text_field( wp_strip_all_tags( $post->post_excerpt ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Repair. Dry run unless $apply is true.
	 *
	 * @param bool          $apply   Write changes.
	 * @param callable|null $logger  Called with ( ID, value ) for each post.
	 * @return int Posts (that would be) repaired.
	 */
	public function repair( $apply = false, ?callable $logger = null ) {
		$count = 0;

		foreach ( $this->audit() as $row ) {
			if ( $apply ) {
				update_post_meta( $row['ID'], 'news_uk_standfirst', $row['standfirst'] );
				update_post_meta( $row['ID'], '_news_uk_repaired', gmdate( 'c' ) ); // Audit trail for rollback.
			}
			if ( $logger ) {
				$logger( $row['ID'], $row['standfirst'] );
			}
			++$count;
		}

		return $count;
	}
}
PHPUnit: dry run changes nothing; apply is idempotent plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q31-incident-cli/tests/class-standfirst-repair-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q31: the repair is safe: audit and dry run change nothing, apply fixes only
 * affected posts, and a second run finds nothing (idempotent).
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use News_UK_Practice\Q31\Standfirst_Repair;
use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * Standfirst repair tests.
 */
class Standfirst_Repair_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Affected posts.
	 *
	 * @var int[]
	 */
	private $broken = [];

	/**
	 * Fine post that must not be touched.
	 *
	 * @var int
	 */
	private $fine;

	/**
	 * Three broken posts, one fine one, one with no excerpt.
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		$this->broken = self::factory()->post->create_many( 3, [ 'post_excerpt' => 'From the <b>old app</b>' ] );
		$this->fine   = self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_excerpt' => 'Excerpt' ] );
		update_post_meta( $this->fine, 'news_uk_standfirst', 'Already set' );
		self::factory()->post->create( [ 'post_excerpt' => '' ] );
	}

	/**
	 * Audit finds exactly the broken posts and proposes clean text.
	 */
	public function test_audit_reports_only_affected_posts() {
		$rows = ( new Standfirst_Repair() )->audit();

		$this->assertEqualsCanonicalizing( $this->broken, wp_list_pluck( $rows, 'ID' ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'From the old app', $rows[0]['standfirst'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Dry run writes nothing.
	 */
	public function test_dry_run_changes_nothing() {
		$count = ( new Standfirst_Repair() )->repair( false );

		$this->assertSame( 3, $count );
		$this->assertSame( '', get_post_meta( $this->broken[0], 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Apply fixes affected posts, leaves others, and records an audit trail.
	 */
	public function test_apply_repairs_and_is_idempotent() {
		$repair = new Standfirst_Repair();
		$repair->repair( true );

		$this->assertSame( 'From the old app', get_post_meta( $this->broken[0], 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
		$this->assertNotEmpty( get_post_meta( $this->broken[0], '_news_uk_repaired', true ) );
		$this->assertSame( 'Already set', get_post_meta( $this->fine, 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
		$this->assertSame( 0, $repair->repair( true ), 'Second run finds nothing.' );
	}

	/**
	 * Batching walks past the batch size without missing posts.
	 */
	public function test_batches_cover_everything() {
		self::factory()->post->create_many( Standfirst_Repair::BATCH + 5, [ 'post_excerpt' => 'x' ] );

		$this->assertCount( Standfirst_Repair::BATCH + 8, ( new Standfirst_Repair() )->audit() );
	}
}

...
Success: 3 posts repaired.

$ wp news-uk standfirst audit

Q32.How do you work with client stakeholders when requirements are unclear or keep changing?

"Pin down the outcome they want, write it down, offer options with a recommendation, deliver in small pieces, and raise risks early with evidence."

Picture thisA taxi passenger who says "somewhere nice by the sea". You don't just start driving: you ask what "nice" means, confirm the destination on the map (written criteria), offer two routes with times (options), and if there's a traffic jam you tell them now, not at the end.
  1. Clarify the outcome and constraints.
  2. Write it down: acceptance criteria, a short brief, a diagram.
  3. Options with trade-offs and a recommendation.
  4. Agree scope in small increments; short, written, regular updates.
  5. Escalate risks early with evidence, not surprises at the deadline.
Your storyAt Ziff Davis you shaped direction through diagrams, technical briefs, epic planning, acceptance criteria and Dev Notes. Pick one concrete example.

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q32-acceptance-criteria/

Turning a vague request ("make exclusives stand out more") into written acceptance criteria, then into tests named after them. This shows "write it down" in a form a principal engineer can run.

acceptance-criteria.md plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q32-acceptance-criteria/acceptance-criteria.md
# Exclusive headline label: acceptance criteria

Request (as first received): "Can we make exclusives stand out more?"

After clarifying with the desk editor (outcome: readers notice exclusives;
constraint: no new block, editors must not break the design):

1. An editor can mark any heading as Exclusive from the Styles panel.
2. An Exclusive heading is shown in the accent colour with a left border.
3. Changing the style back to Default removes the treatment.
4. No custom block is added (core Heading + block style).

Out of scope for this increment: an "EXCLUSIVE" text badge (option B, needs
design sign-off). Each numbered criterion is a test in `exclusive.spec.js`.
exclusive.spec.js: one test per criterion plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q32-acceptance-criteria/exclusive.spec.js
/**
 * Q32: acceptance criteria, written down, then made executable.
 * Each test name is one numbered criterion from acceptance-criteria.md.
 */

/**
 * WordPress dependencies
 */
const { test, expect } = require( '@wordpress/e2e-test-utils-playwright' );

const ACCENT = 'rgb(200, 16, 46)'; // --wp--preset--color--accent (#c8102e).

test.describe( 'Exclusive headline label', () => {
	test.afterEach( async ( { requestUtils } ) => {
		await requestUtils.deleteAllPosts();
	} );

	test( 'AC1+AC4: editor marks a core heading as Exclusive from the Styles panel', async ( {
		admin,
		editor,
		page,
	} ) => {
		await admin.createNewPost();
		await editor.insertBlock( {
			name: 'core/heading',
			attributes: { content: 'Minister resigns' },
		} );

		// In WordPress 6.9 block styles are a "Styles" panel in the Block tab of
		// the sidebar (older versions had a separate Styles tab).
		await editor.openDocumentSettingsSidebar();
		await page
			.getByRole( 'region', { name: 'Editor settings' } )
			.getByRole( 'button', { name: 'Exclusive', exact: true } )
			.click();

		await expect.poll( editor.getBlocks ).toMatchObject( [
			{
				name: 'core/heading', // AC4: still a core block.
				attributes: { className: 'is-style-exclusive' },
			},
		] );
	} );

	test( 'AC2: readers see the accent colour and left border', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		const post = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			status: 'publish',
			title: 'AC2',
			content:
				'<!-- wp:heading {"className":"is-style-exclusive"} --><h2 class="wp-block-heading is-style-exclusive">Minister resigns</h2><!-- /wp:heading -->',
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ post.id }` );
		const heading = page.getByRole( 'heading', {
			name: 'Minister resigns',
		} );

		await expect( heading ).toHaveCSS( 'color', ACCENT );
		await expect( heading ).toHaveCSS( 'border-left-color', ACCENT );
		await expect( heading ).toHaveCSS( 'border-left-width', '6px' );
	} );

	test( 'AC3: switching back to Default removes the treatment', async ( {
		page,
		requestUtils,
	} ) => {
		const post = await requestUtils.createPost( {
			status: 'publish',
			title: 'AC3',
			content:
				'<!-- wp:heading --><h2 class="wp-block-heading">Minister resigns</h2><!-- /wp:heading -->',
		} );

		await page.goto( `/?p=${ post.id }` );

		await expect(
			page.getByRole( 'heading', { name: 'Minister resigns' } )
		).not.toHaveCSS( 'color', ACCENT );
	} );
} );

How it's tested: All three criteria tests pass in Chrome. The feature under test is the Q7 "Exclusive" heading style.

Q33.How do you handle disagreement in a code review, or with a principal engineer's decision?

"Separate facts from preferences. Back facts with the standard or data, and let preferences go. After two rounds of comments, switch to a call. The principal engineer owns direction: I raise concerns with evidence, then commit."

Picture thisTabs vs spaces is a preference: let the author choose. An unescaped output is a fact: point to the standard. Arguing preferences in 20 comments is like two people holding a door open for each other forever.
  • Fact (a standard, security, measurable performance) means link the rule or show the numbers.
  • Preference means the author decides.
  • Two rounds, then talk. Record the outcome in the PR.
  • Respect ownership: at News UK the principal engineers set direction. Disagree with evidence, then commit and make it work.
  • Big ones: write a short ADR. Escalate decisions, not people.
Remember it as"Fact or taste? Two rounds, then talk. Disagree, then commit."
Real code in this repoNo code: this is about people. Here's how a "fact, not taste" comment looks with Conventional Comments, backed by the standard (the PHPCS output from Q9):
issue (blocking): this echoes $_GET['s'] unescaped (XSS). PHPCS flags it as
WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped. Could we wrap it in esc_html()?

question (non-blocking): would a pattern of core blocks cover this instead of a new
custom block? If we did that, would editors lose anything they need?

nitpick (non-blocking): I'd usually name this $post_ids, but happy either way.

Q35.How have you approached general AI adoption and integration?

"I use AI as an assistant for scaffolding, test stubs, explaining legacy code and docs, not for designing core logic. I follow the client's policy, which here means GitHub Copilot, and AI output goes through the same standards, tests and review as anything else."

Picture thisAI is a fast junior who never gets tired but sometimes confidently makes things up. You give it the boring parts, and you review everything it hands back as carefully as you'd review a new starter's PR.
  • Daily use: scaffolding, test stubs, explaining legacy code, drafting docs. Core logic stays human-designed.
  • Guardrails: follow the client policy (News UK provides GitHub Copilot and doesn't permit other assistants on client code). No secrets or proprietary code in unapproved tools. AI output is untrusted: PHPCS, VIP standards, tests and human review all still apply.
  • In the product: summaries, tagging, alt-text suggestions via server-side API calls with caching and rate limits, and an editor approves before anything is published.
  • As a lead: agree team conventions and review AI-assisted PRs with the same rigour.
Remember it as"Approved tool, untrusted output, human in the loop."

Real code in this repo

📁 plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q35-ai-summary/

AI in the product, with every guardrail from the answer. An editor asks for a suggested standfirst, and the server calls the Claude API, so the key never reaches the browser. The suggestion is saved as a private draft. Only an editor with publish rights can approve it into the public field, and the approver is recorded. The output is treated as untrusted (sanitised and length-capped). Refusals and API errors never publish anything, the same text isn't billed twice, and there's a per-user rate limit. Locally a fake endpoint returns the same response shape, so it can be demoed without a key.

client.php: request + careful response parsing plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q35-ai-summary/client.php
function suggest_standfirst( $title, $content ) {
	if ( '' === api_key() ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_not_configured', __( 'AI summaries are not configured on this environment.', 'news-uk' ), [ 'status' => 503 ] );
	}

	$response = wp_remote_post(
		(string) apply_filters( 'news_uk_ai_endpoint', API_URL ),
		[
			// VIP flags timeouts over 3s because a slow API would tie up PHP workers
			// serving readers. This call is only made from an editor's REST request
			// (never on a front-end page), and LLM responses take several seconds.
			'timeout' => 60, // phpcs:ignore WordPressVIPMinimum.Performance.RemoteRequestTimeout.timeout_timeout
			'headers' => [
				'Content-Type'      => 'application/json',
				'x-api-key'         => api_key(),
				'anthropic-version' => API_VERSION,
				// If a safety classifier declines, the API retries on a suitable model.
				'anthropic-beta'    => 'server-side-fallback-2026-07-01',
			],
			'body'    => wp_json_encode(
				[
					'model'         => MODEL,
					'max_tokens'    => 4000,
					'fallbacks'     => 'default',
					'output_config' => [ 'effort' => 'low' ], // Short, simple task.
					'system'        => SYSTEM_PROMPT,
					'messages'      => [
						[
							'role'    => 'user',
							'content' => "Title: {$title}\n\nArticle:\n{$content}",
						],
					],
				]
			),
		]
	);

	return parse_response( $response );
}

/**
 * Turn the HTTP response into the suggestion text, or a WP_Error.
 *
 * @param array|\WP_Error $response wp_remote_post() result.
 * @return string|\WP_Error
 */
function parse_response( $response ) {
	if ( is_wp_error( $response ) ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_http', $response->get_error_message(), [ 'status' => 502 ] );
	}

	$code = wp_remote_retrieve_response_code( $response );
	$body = json_decode( wp_remote_retrieve_body( $response ), true );

	if ( 200 !== $code ) {
		$status = 429 === $code ? 429 : 502; // Pass rate limits through so the UI can say "try later".
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_api', $body['error']['message'] ?? 'API error ' . $code, [ 'status' => $status ] );
	}

	// Always check why the model stopped before reading the content.
	$stop_reason = $body['stop_reason'] ?? '';
	if ( 'refusal' === $stop_reason ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_refusal', __( 'The model declined to summarise this article.', 'news-uk' ), [ 'status' => 422 ] );
	}
	if ( 'end_turn' !== $stop_reason ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_incomplete', 'Unexpected stop_reason: ' . $stop_reason, [ 'status' => 502 ] );
	}

	// Content is a list of blocks (thinking blocks may come first): keep the text ones.
	$text = '';
	foreach ( (array) ( $body['content'] ?? [] ) as $block ) {
		if ( 'text' === ( $block['type'] ?? '' ) ) {
			$text .= $block['text'];
		}
	}

	// Untrusted output: plain text only, capped.
	$text = wp_trim_words( sanitize_text_field( $text ), 40, '…' );

	return '' === $text ? new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_empty', 'Empty response', [ 'status' => 502 ] ) : $text;
}
routes.php: draft first, human approves plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q35-ai-summary/routes.php
function approve_draft( \WP_REST_Request $request ) {
	$post_id = (int) $request['id'];
	$draft   = get_post_meta( $post_id, DRAFT_META, true );

	if ( '' === $draft ) {
		return new \WP_Error( 'news_uk_ai_no_draft', __( 'There is no suggestion to approve.', 'news-uk' ), [ 'status' => 409 ] );
	}

	update_post_meta( $post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', $draft );
	update_post_meta( $post_id, APPROVED_META, get_current_user_id() ); // Who signed it off.
	delete_post_meta( $post_id, DRAFT_META );

	return [ 'published' => get_post_meta( $post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) ];
}
PHPUnit: API mocked with pre_http_request plugins/news-uk-practice/src/q35-ai-summary/tests/class-ai-summary-test.php
<?php
/**
 * Q35: the AI flow, with the API mocked at the HTTP layer.
 *
 * @package News_UK_Practice
 */

namespace News_UK_Practice\Tests;

use WP_REST_Request;
use WP_UnitTestCase;

/**
 * AI summary tests.
 */
class AI_Summary_Test extends WP_UnitTestCase {

	/**
	 * Captured outgoing requests.
	 *
	 * @var array[]
	 */
	private $calls = [];

	/**
	 * Post under test.
	 *
	 * @var int
	 */
	private $post_id;

	/**
	 * Fake key, an editor, a post, and a default "API".
	 */
	public function set_up() {
		parent::set_up();
		wp_cache_flush_group( 'news_uk_ai' );
		remove_all_filters( 'news_uk_ai_endpoint' );
		add_filter( 'news_uk_ai_api_key', fn() => 'test-key' );
		wp_set_current_user( self::factory()->user->create( [ 'role' => 'editor' ] ) );
		$this->post_id = self::factory()->post->create(
			[
				'post_title'   => 'Rates decision',
				'post_content' => '<p>The Bank of England held interest rates at 4% on Thursday.</p>',
			]
		);
		$this->fake_api( 200, $this->message( 'end_turn', 'The Bank of England held rates at 4%.' ) );
	}

	/**
	 * A Messages API response body.
	 *
	 * @param string $stop_reason Stop reason.
	 * @param string $text        Text block.
	 * @return array
	 */
	private function message( $stop_reason, $text ) {
		return [
			'type'        => 'message',
			'content'     => [
				[
					'type'     => 'thinking',
					'thinking' => '',
				],
				[
					'type' => 'text',
					'text' => $text,
				],
			],
			'stop_reason' => $stop_reason,
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Make the API return $code / $body.
	 *
	 * @param int   $code HTTP status.
	 * @param array $body JSON body.
	 */
	private function fake_api( $code, $body ) {
		remove_all_filters( 'pre_http_request' );
		add_filter(
			'pre_http_request',
			function ( $pre, $args, $url ) use ( $code, $body ) {
				$this->calls[] = [
					'url'  => $url,
					'args' => $args,
					'body' => json_decode( $args['body'], true ),
				];
				return [
					'response' => [ 'code' => $code ],
					'body'     => wp_json_encode( $body ),
				];
			},
			10,
			3
		);
	}

	/**
	 * Call a route.
	 *
	 * @param string $suffix '' or '/approve'.
	 * @return \WP_REST_Response
	 */
	private function post( $suffix = '' ) {
		return rest_do_request( new WP_REST_Request( 'POST', '/news-uk/v1/ai-summary/' . $this->post_id . $suffix ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Request goes to the Messages API with the right headers, model and full text.
	 */
	public function test_request_shape() {
		$this->post();

		$call = $this->calls[0];
		$this->assertSame( 'https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages', $call['url'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'test-key', $call['args']['headers']['x-api-key'] );
		$this->assertSame( '2026-10-21', $call['args']['headers']['anthropic-version'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'claude-opus-5-5', $call['body']['model'] );
		$this->assertSame( 'default', $call['body']['fallbacks'] );
		$this->assertStringContainsString( 'held interest rates at 4% on Thursday', $call['body']['messages'][0]['content'] );
	}

	/**
	 * Suggestion is a DRAFT: readers see nothing until approved.
	 */
	public function test_suggestion_is_saved_as_draft_only() {
		$response = $this->post();

		$this->assertSame( 200, $response->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'The Bank of England held rates at 4%.', $response->get_data()['draft'] );
		$this->assertSame( '', get_post_meta( $this->post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Approval publishes it and records who approved.
	 */
	public function test_approve_publishes_and_records_approver() {
		$this->post();
		$response = $this->post( '/approve' );

		$this->assertSame( 200, $response->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 'The Bank of England held rates at 4%.', get_post_meta( $this->post_id, 'news_uk_standfirst', true ) );
		$this->assertSame( get_current_user_id(), (int) get_post_meta( $this->post_id, '_news_uk_ai_standfirst_approved_by', true ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Contributors can ask for a suggestion on their own draft but not publish it.
	 */
	public function test_contributor_cannot_approve() {
		$contributor = self::factory()->user->create( [ 'role' => 'contributor' ] );
		wp_set_current_user( $contributor );
		$this->post_id = self::factory()->post->create(
			[
				'post_author'  => $contributor,
				'post_status'  => 'draft',
				'post_content' => 'Some text.',
			]
		);

		$this->assertSame( 200, $this->post()->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( 403, $this->post( '/approve' )->get_status() );
	}

	/**
	 * Same content twice = one API call.
	 */
	public function test_same_content_is_not_billed_twice() {
		$this->post();
		$this->post();

		$this->assertCount( 1, $this->calls );
	}

	/**
	 * Refusals and API errors never publish anything.
	 *
	 * @dataProvider failures
	 *
	 * @param int   $code   HTTP status.
	 * @param array $body   Body.
	 * @param int   $status Expected REST status.
	 */
	public function test_failures_publish_nothing( $code, $body, $status ) {
		$this->fake_api( $code, $body );

		$this->assertSame( $status, $this->post()->get_status() );
		$this->assertSame( '', get_post_meta( $this->post_id, '_news_uk_ai_standfirst_draft', true ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Failure cases.
	 *
	 * @return array
	 */
	public static function failures() {
		return [
			'refusal'    => [
				200,
				[
					'content'     => [],
					'stop_reason' => 'refusal',
				],
				422,
			],
			'rate limit' => [ 429, [ 'error' => [ 'message' => 'rate_limit_error' ] ], 429 ],
			'overloaded' => [ 529, [ 'error' => [ 'message' => 'overloaded_error' ] ], 502 ],
			'max_tokens' => [
				200,
				[
					'content'     => [],
					'stop_reason' => 'max_tokens',
				],
				502,
			],
		];
	}

	/**
	 * Output is untrusted: HTML stripped, length capped.
	 */
	public function test_output_is_sanitised() {
		$this->fake_api( 200, $this->message( 'end_turn', '<script>x</script>Rates held. ' . str_repeat( 'word ', 100 ) ) );

		$draft = $this->post()->get_data()['draft'];

		$this->assertStringNotContainsString( '<', $draft );
		$this->assertLessThanOrEqual( 41, str_word_count( $draft ) );
	}

	/**
	 * Per-user rate limit protects cost.
	 */
	public function test_rate_limited_per_user() {
		foreach ( range( 1, 5 ) as $i ) {
			wp_update_post(
				[
					'ID'           => $this->post_id,
					'post_content' => "Version $i of the story.",
				]
			);
			$this->assertSame( 200, $this->post()->get_status() );
		}

		wp_update_post(
			[
				'ID'           => $this->post_id,
				'post_content' => 'Version 6.',
			]
		);
		$this->assertSame( 429, $this->post()->get_status() );
	}

	/**
	 * No key configured: clear 503, no HTTP call.
	 */
	public function test_not_configured() {
		remove_all_filters( 'news_uk_ai_api_key' );

		$this->assertSame( 503, $this->post()->get_status() );
		$this->assertCount( 0, $this->calls );
	}
}

Seen running: Run from a logged-in browser against the real REST routes (fake model locally): the draft came back while the public standfirst stayed empty; approval published it; an anonymous request got 401. Side lesson: VIP sends Cache-Control: max-age=60 on anonymous REST GETs, so readers can see the old value for up to a minute after an edit.

How it's tested: This uses wp_remote_post rather than the Anthropic PHP SDK because VIP wants outbound calls on the WP HTTP API: platform timeouts, logging, and mocking in tests. The model is claude-opus-5-5, with server-side fallbacks if a safety classifier declines. The 60s timeout breaks a VIP rule on purpose (editor-only request), with the reason in a comment.
